Notation

Physical AI draws on three disciplines at once. Machine learning supplies the foundation models, vision-language-action architectures, diffusion policies, and action chunking that propose what a robot should do. Robotics and control supply the mechanics that decide what it can do: articulated dynamics, the Lie group \(\SpecialEuclideanGroup\), contact and friction, Lyapunov stability, control barrier functions, and actuator electromechanics. Embedded and real-time systems supply the machinery that decides when it happens, from interconnect and memory contention through zero-order hold, real-time scheduling, leases, hardware watchdogs, and functional safety.

Because each discipline developed its mathematical conventions independently over decades, combining them creates symbol collisions that cyber-physical systems engineering has long lived with. For example, writing \(\nabla_{\Glyph{\theta}} \Policy(\Glyph{\mathbf{a}} \mid \Glyph{\mathbf{q}})\) when \(\Glyph{\theta}\) also denotes joint angles, or writing \(\Glyph{\mathbf{a}}_t\) for an action when \(\Glyph{\mathbf{a}}\) denotes Cartesian acceleration or barrier constraints, confuses spatial mechanics with parameter optimization and control execution authority.

The sections that follow state the cross-discipline disambiguation rules, the governing relationships of embodied systems, and the symbol tables used throughout this book. Every symbol of the series core keeps its foundational meaning: \(\Time\) is time, \(\Bandwidth\) bandwidth, \(\Latency\) latency, \(\PeakRate\) peak rate, \(\Operations\) operations, \(\ArithmeticIntensity\) arithmetic intensity, \(\DataVolume\) bytes moved, \(\LossFunction\) loss, \(\LearningRate\) learning rate, and \(\mathcal{O}(\cdot)\) asymptotic cost. Serving and memory arithmetic on the robot’s onboard computer uses those symbols, with a subscript naming the instance (\(\SustainedMemoryBandwidth\), \(\PrefillOperations\)).

Cross-Discipline Disambiguation

When physical plant dynamics meet deep neural networks and real-time embedded hardware, common mathematical shorthand breaks down. The conventions below resolve these cross-domain collisions systematically:

Symbol Canonical Convention Colliding Meaning Disambiguation Rule
\(\Glyph{\theta}\) Learned model (policy) parameters, as in \(\Policy\) Joint angle, trajectory polynomial \(\Glyph{\theta}(\Glyph{t})\), heading, azimuth, attitude or mounting error, clock offset, or thermal resistance \(\Glyph{\theta_{JA}}\) Bare \(\Glyph{\theta}\) is always the parameter collection of a learned model, set italic (never bold), as the series core defines it. A geometric angle always carries a subscript that names it: \(\JointAngle\) for one joint (\(\Glyph{\theta_{\text{motor}}}\) and \(\Glyph{\theta_{\text{link}}}\) on either side of a reducer, \(\EncoderAngle\) for an encoder reading), \(\HeadingAngle\) for planar heading, \(\AzimuthAngle\) for azimuth, \(\AttitudeError\) for attitude error, \(\ExtrinsicMisalignmentAngle\) for extrinsic misalignment, \(\TiltAngle\) for tilt from vertical, \(\OrientationTolerance\) for an orientation tolerance, and \(\ElectricalRotorAngle\) for the electrical rotor angle; increments keep the subscript (\(\FiniteChange\JointAngle\), \(\SmallChange\EncoderAngle\)). Joint configurations are the generalized coordinates \(\GeneralizedCoordinates \in \mathbb{R}^\GeneralizedCoordinateCount\) with entries \(\JointCoordinate_i\), and a joint trajectory polynomial is \(\JointCoordinate(\TimeInstant) = \sum \PolynomialTrajectoryCoefficient_k \TimeInstant^{\Glyph{k}}\) with boundary values \(\JointCoordinate_0, \JointCoordinate_1\) (never \(\Glyph{\theta}(\Glyph{t})\)). A clock offset is \(\ClockOffset\) (in seconds). Thermal resistance is \(\ThermalResistance\) or \(\JunctionAmbientThermalResistance\) (in \(\text{K/W}\)), never \(\Glyph{\theta_{JA}}\).
\(\Glyph{\mathbf{a}}\) vs. \(\NominalControlCommand\) vs. \(\CertifiedControlAction\) vs. \(\JointTorque\) Four-stage authority chain (\(\CognitivePolicyAction_{t} \to \NominalControlCommand \to \CertifiedControlAction \to \JointTorque\)) Generic “action” symbol colliding with deceleration \(\CredibleBrakingDeceleration\) or a barrier constraint row Disambiguate execution authority: \(\CognitivePolicyAction_{t}\) is the unprivileged policy action (dimensionless/normalized); \(\NominalControlCommand\) is the proposed plant control setpoint; \(\CertifiedControlAction \in \AdmissibleControlSet\) is the certified, CBF-filtered control command; \(\JointTorque \in \mathbb{R}^\GeneralizedCoordinateCount\) is realized physical actuator torque. Control commands are bold \(\ControlCommand\) (with time index \(\ControlCommand_t\)). Credible linear deceleration is \(\CredibleBrakingDeceleration\) (\(\text{m/s}^2\)); stacked barrier constraints are \(\Glyph{\mathbf{A}_{\text{cbf}}}(\MechanicalPlantState)\ControlCommand \le \Glyph{\mathbf{b}_{\text{cbf}}}(\MechanicalPlantState)\), and a single constraint row is \(\BarrierConstraintNormal(\MechanicalPlantState)^\top\ControlCommand \le \BarrierConstraintBound(\MechanicalPlantState)\).
\(\JointTorque\) vs. \(\Glyph{\tau}\) Actuator torque vector \(\JointTorque \in \mathbb{R}^\GeneralizedCoordinateCount\) (\(\text{N}\cdot\text{m}\) or \(\text{N}\)) Time constants (\(\ElectricalTimeConstant\), \(\ThermalTimeConstant\)), delays (\(\PrebrakeDelay\), \(\ZohTransportDelay\)), observation age, lease durations, or real-time task names Bare or bold \(\Glyph{\tau}\) is always torque (\(\JointTorque \in \mathbb{R}^\GeneralizedCoordinateCount\), scalar joint torque \(\CommandedJointTorque_i\), component torques such as \(\MotorTorque\), \(\HoldingTorque\), \(\Glyph{\tau_{\text{bias}}}\)), with \(\MotorTorque = \MotorTorqueConstant \TorqueProducingCurrent\) at the motor. A subscripted Greek \(\Glyph{\tau}\) marks a time only for these named constants and delays: \(\PrebrakeDelay\) (pre-brake delay, excluding \(\DispatchAdmissionInterval\)) and its ceiling \(\PrebrakeDelayCeiling\), \(\ElectricalTimeConstant = \PhaseInductance/\PhaseResistance\) (electrical), \(\ThermalTimeConstant = \ThermalResistance \StatorThermalCapacitance\) (thermal, with stage constants such as \(\CopperThermalTimeConstant\) and \(\CaseThermalTimeConstant\)), \(\Glyph{\tau_{\text{fall}}}\) (balance divergence), \(\ZohTransportDelay\) (sample-and-hold delay), \(\EvidenceHorizon\) (evidence horizon), and \(\BlendingTransitionWindow\) (authority blend window). Every other interval uses Latin \(\Glyph{T}\) or \(\Glyph{t}\): observation age is \(\ObservationAge\), never \(\Glyph{\tau}\); leases are \(\ChunkLease\), \(\ManualLease\), and \(\IntentAdministrativeLease\); compute and fieldbus times are \(\EnforcerComputeTime\) and \(\CommandTransportTime\); a pure transport delay is \(\PureTransportDelay\). A real-time task is named “task \(\Glyph{i}\)”, never \(\Glyph{\tau}_i\).
\(\MechanicalPlantState\) vs. \(\Glyph{s}\) vs. \(\CartesianPosition\) vs. \(\VitImagePatch\) Mechanical plant state \(\MechanicalPlantState = [\GeneralizedCoordinates^\top, \GeneralizedVelocities^\top]^\top \in \mathbb{R}^{2\GeneralizedCoordinateCount}\) World state \(\Glyph{s} \in \Glyph{\mathcal{S}}\), 3D position \(\CartesianPosition = [\CartesianXCoordinate, \CartesianYCoordinate, \CartesianZCoordinate]^\top\), image point, or ViT image patch \(\VitImagePatch\) Continuous mechanical plant state is bold vector \(\MechanicalPlantState \in \mathbb{R}^{2\GeneralizedCoordinateCount}\). Ground-truth latent world state is italic \(\PhysicalWorldState_{t}\) (a mixed-type state, never bold). 3D Cartesian position is vector \(\CartesianPosition \in \mathbb{R}^3\), including a camera-frame point \(\CameraFramePoint\) and a voxel center \(\VoxelCenterPosition\); the translation of a rigid transform is \(\RigidTransformTranslation\). An image point in homogeneous pixel coordinates is \(\HomogeneousPixelCoordinates = [\PixelColumnCoordinate, \PixelRowCoordinate, 1]^\top\), never \(\Glyph{\mathbf{x}}\) or \(\Glyph{\mathbf{u}}\). Vision transformer patch embeddings use explicitly subscripted \(\VitImagePatch\) or latent feature \(\VitPatchTokenEmbedding\).
\(\Parameters\) Model parameter count \(\Parameters\) (series core) Power (\(\WindingLossPower, \Glyph{P_{\text{peak}}}\)), estimation covariance \(\EstimationErrorCovariance\), failure probability or rate, tail-latency percentile, or 3D point \(\CartesianPosition\) Use \(\Parameters\) for parameter count. Electrical/mechanical power always carries a subscript (\(\WindingLossPower, \Glyph{P_{\text{peak}}}, \HeatDissipationPower\), in Watts); the estimator’s error covariance is bold matrix \(\EstimationErrorCovariance\), and a generic Gaussian covariance is \(\GaussianCovarianceMatrix\); a per-trial probability is lowercase italic \(\Glyph{p}\) (\(\TrialFailureProbability, \TargetTrialSuccessProbability\)), a rate per hour is \(\Glyph{\lambda}\) (\(\SystemHazardRate, \TargetHazardRate\)), and an event probability is \(\Pr(\cdot)\); percentiles are upright p50, p95, and p99 (never \(\Glyph{P}_{99}\)); 3D Cartesian position is bold lowercase \(\CartesianPosition = [\CartesianXCoordinate, \CartesianYCoordinate, \CartesianZCoordinate]^\top\).
\(\BatchSize\) Batch size \(\BatchSize\) (series core) Bandwidth (\(\Bandwidth\)), Body frame (\(\BodyReferenceFrame\)), bus transfer size, SPRT boundary, or input matrix \(\StateSpaceInputMatrix\) Batch size is \(\BatchSize\); sustained bandwidth is \(\Bandwidth\) (per the single-device decomposition), with a subscript naming the path (\(\SustainedMemoryBandwidth\), \(\Glyph{\Bandwidth_{\text{chirp}}}\)); coordinate reference frames use calligraphic script (\(\WorldReferenceFrame, \BodyReferenceFrame, \Glyph{\mathcal{F}_E}\)); the bytes of bus transaction \(\Glyph{k}\) are \({\DataVolume}_{\Glyph{k}}\); SPRT boundaries are \(\SprtUpperBoundary\) and \(\SprtLowerBoundary\); state-space control input matrix is bold capital \(\StateSpaceInputMatrix \in \mathbb{R}^{\StateDimension \times \ControlInputDimension}\).
\(\BumplessTransferBlend\) Bumpless transfer blending function \(\BumplessTransferBlend(\BumplessTransferProgress) \in [0, 1]\) CBF decay (\(\LinearBarrierDecayRate\)), significance (\(\HypothesisTestSignificanceLevel\)), angular acceleration (\(\AngularAcceleration\)), copper tempco (\(\CopperTemperatureCoefficient\)), or noise schedule (\(\DiffusionCumulativeSchedule_{k}\)) Authority transfer uses scalar function \(\BumplessTransferBlend(\BumplessTransferProgress) \in [0, 1]\) of normalized time \(\BumplessTransferProgress \in [0, 1]\); barrier linear decay uses \(\LinearBarrierDecayRate > 0\); statistical confidence significance uses \(\HypothesisTestSignificanceLevel = 1 - \text{CL}\); angular acceleration is bold vector \(\AngularAcceleration \in \mathbb{R}^3\) (\(\text{rad/s}^2\)); copper resistance tempco is \(\CopperTemperatureCoefficient\); diffusion noise schedules use indexed \(\DiffusionCumulativeSchedule_{k}\).
\(\CoriolisCentrifugalMatrix\) Coriolis matrix \(\CoriolisCentrifugalMatrix(\GeneralizedCoordinates, \GeneralizedVelocities) \in \mathbb{R}^{\GeneralizedCoordinateCount \times \GeneralizedCoordinateCount}\) Safe set \(\ForwardInvariantSafeSet\), conformal prediction set, assurance claim, state-space output matrix, controllability matrix, capacitance (\(\StatorThermalCapacitance, \DcBusBulkCapacitance\)), CoM \(\MassCenterPosition\), or shield coverage \(\ShieldInterceptionCoverage\) Coriolis/centrifugal forces use matrix function \(\CoriolisCentrifugalMatrix(\GeneralizedCoordinates, \GeneralizedVelocities)\), always written with its arguments; forward-invariant safe sets use calligraphic \(\ForwardInvariantSafeSet \subset \mathbb{R}^{2\GeneralizedCoordinateCount}\), defined on the plant state, and the conformal prediction set \(\ConformalPredictionSet\) of target grounding shares that glyph but never appears beside a safe set; the top-level claim of an assurance argument is italic \(\AssuranceClaim\); the state-space output map is \(\MeasurementMatrix\) (the same map as the Kalman measurement map), never \(\Glyph{\mathbf{C}}\); the controllability matrix is \(\ControllabilityMatrix\); electrical/thermal capacitance uses italic \(\Glyph{C}\) with a subscript; center of mass is bold vector \(\MassCenterPosition \in \mathbb{R}^3\); safety shield coverage of hazard class \(\HazardClassIndex\) uses \(\ShieldInterceptionCoverage \in [0, 1]\).
\(\ActionChunk\) Action chunk \(\ActionChunk_t\), \(\Glyph{\mathbf{A}_{t:t+H}}\), and its denoising iterate \(\ActionChunk_t^\DiffusionStepIndex\) Continuous LTI state matrix, barrier constraint matrix Bold \(\ActionChunk\) with a time or denoising index is the action chunk. The continuous LTI state matrix is \(\StateSpaceSystemMatrix\) (its discretizations \(\ForwardEulerTransitionMatrix\) and \(\BackwardEulerTransitionMatrix\), the discrete Kalman transition \(\StateTransitionMatrix\)); stacked barrier constraints use \(\Glyph{\mathbf{A}_{\text{cbf}}}\).
\(\Velocity\) Velocity (\(\Velocity\), end-effector velocity \(\Glyph{\mathbf{v}_e}\)) Measurement noise, voxel Bold \(\Velocity\) is a velocity. Measurement noise is \(\MeasurementNoiseVector\) with covariance \(\MeasurementNoiseCovariance\) (bold \(\Glyph{\mathbf{R}}\) is reserved for rotation); a voxel is addressed by its center \(\VoxelCenterPosition\).
\(\CameraIntrinsicCalibrationMatrix\) Camera intrinsic calibration matrix \(\CameraIntrinsicCalibrationMatrix \in \mathbb{R}^{3 \times 3}\) Contact stiffness (\(\ContactStiffness\)), control gains (\(\PidProportionalGain, \PidDerivativeGain\), LQR gain), motor constants (\(\MotorTorqueConstant, \MotorBackEmfConstant\)), ensembling window \(\EnsemblingWindowLength\), or diffusion step \(\DiffusionStepIndex\) Camera intrinsics use bold matrix \(\CameraIntrinsicCalibrationMatrix\); contact stiffness is \(\ContactStiffness\), with body-region stiffness \(\HertzianContactStiffness\) (\(\text{N/m}\)); feedback control gains use capitalized scalars \(\PidProportionalGain, \PidDerivativeGain\), and a state-feedback gain matrix is \(\LqrFeedbackGain\); motor torque/back-EMF constants use \(\MotorTorqueConstant, \MotorBackEmfConstant\) (per joint \({\MotorTorqueConstant}_{i}\)); temporal ensembling window length uses integer \(\EnsemblingWindowLength\); the number of denoising or solver evaluations per chunk is \(\DecoderEvaluationCount\), never \(\Glyph{K}\); diffusion noise steps use lowercase index \(\DiffusionStepIndex\); the Kalman gain is \(\KalmanGain_{k}\).
\(\GeneralizedInertiaMatrix\) Articulated inertia matrix \(\GeneralizedInertiaMatrix(\GeneralizedCoordinates) \in \mathbb{R}^{\GeneralizedCoordinateCount \times \GeneralizedCoordinateCount}\) Mass (\(\Mass, \EffectiveImpactMass\)), memory footprint (\(\WeightMemory, \KvCacheMemory\)), or fleet size (\(\FleetRobotCount\)) Articulated generalized mass/inertia uses bold matrix \(\GeneralizedInertiaMatrix(\GeneralizedCoordinates)\); physical body mass is lowercase italic \(\Mass\) (\(\text{kg}\)); effective impact mass is \(\EffectiveImpactMass\); hardware memory storage uses capital \(\Glyph{M}\) with descriptive subscript (in bytes); robot fleet size uses \(\FleetRobotCount\).
\(\Time\) Time (series core) Temperature Bare \(\Glyph{T}\) is always time. A temperature always carries a subscript that names where it is measured: \(\WindingTemperature\) (winding), \(\StatorCoreTemperature\), \(\JunctionTemperature\) (junction), \(\CaseTemperature\), \(\AmbientTemperature\) (ambient), \(\WindingTemperatureLimit\) (rated limit), \(\ThrottleTripTemperature\), \(\EquilibriumWindingTemperature\) (equilibrium), and \(\CopperReferenceTemperature\) (the reference temperature of \(\CopperTemperatureCoefficient\)). A temperature rise is \(\JouleanTemperatureRise\) (in \(\text{K}\)); a time interval is \(\FiniteChange \TimeInstant\), never \(\Glyph{\Delta} \Glyph{T}\).
SIL (abbreviation) Safety integrity level (IEC 61508 family), a grade of a safety function Software-in-the-loop, the first rung of the verification ladder Spell out at first use in each chapter. “SIL 2” and similar graded forms always mean safety integrity level; the verification ladder writes software-in-the-loop (SIL) only where the ladder’s abbreviations MIL, SIL, PIL, and HIL appear together, and never beside a safety integrity level.

Governing Relationships

Physical intelligence operates subject to thermodynamic, mechanical, kinematic, and communications constraints. The relationships below state those constraints in the symbols this book uses. They are working relationships, not laws: the book’s four bedrock laws are the ones The Four Bedrock Laws states.

The sense-to-actuation delay budget

Delay converts directly into travel before any retarding force begins. The pre-brake delay \(\PrebrakeDelay\) runs from the capture of the frame that first shows a hazard to retarding force, excluding the dispatch-to-admission interval \(\DispatchAdmissionInterval\), which the stopping budget charges against its timing allowance. The stop it leaves, whose travel is \(\ResidentStopDistance(\SpeedAtDetection)\), must fit inside the clear distance, and the spare that remains must pay for \(\SpeedAtDetection\,\DispatchAdmissionInterval\):

\[\PrebrakeDelay = \ObservationAge + \ChunkLease + \PermissionLoopPeriod + \CommandTransportTime + \ActuatorResponseTime\]

\[\SpeedAtDetection\,\PrebrakeDelay + \ResidentStopDistance(\SpeedAtDetection) + \LocalizationBound + \ProtectiveClearanceMargin + \TrackingErrorBound \le \ClearDistance\]

Inference time is spent on the proposal side of the proposal boundary. It ages the evidence behind a proposal, which the permission path checks against the proposal’s expiry. It is neither a term of \(\PrebrakeDelay\) nor part of the permission loop’s period \(\PermissionLoopPeriod\), because the fallback does not wait for the proposer.

The appendices regroup the same interval for two specific stop paths, and each form defines its own terms. For a stop triggered by lease expiry, \(\PrebrakeDelay = \ChunkLease + \DetectionInterval + \FallbackHold + \ObservationAge + \ActuatorResponseTime\); for a stop triggered by a hazard detector, \(\PrebrakeDelay = \ObservationAge + \DetectionInterval + \StopDecisionComputeBound + \CommandTransportTime + \ActuatorResponseTime\).

Symbol Definition Unit Notes
\(\PrebrakeDelay\) Pre-Brake Delay \(\text{ms}\) Time from capture of the frame that first shows a hazard to the onset of retarding force, excluding \(\DispatchAdmissionInterval\); the machine travels \(\SpeedAtDetection\,(\PrebrakeDelay + \DispatchAdmissionInterval)\) before braking begins. Its allowed ceiling is \(\PrebrakeDelayCeiling\).
\(\ObservationAge\) Observation Age \(\text{ms}\) Age of the state the permission path acts on, from the exposure midpoint at the sensor to the decision, which enters \(\PrebrakeDelay\). Its perception-side terms are the exposure midpoint \(\tfrac{1}{2}\ExposureDuration\), sensor readout, transfer, and perception processing; a bound on it is \(\Glyph{t_{\text{age},\max}}\).
\(\ChunkLease\) Chunk Lease \(\text{ms}\) Longest interval an admitted chunk may keep driving the actuators without renewal, which enters \(\PrebrakeDelay\); chosen, then checked against the ceiling the stopping budget derives.
\(\ChunkRenewalPeriod\) Chunk Renewal Period \(\text{ms}\) Interval between successful admissions of the chunk policy; with \(\RenewalJitterAllowance\) it sets the floor of the lease rule, \(\ChunkRenewalPeriod + \RenewalJitterAllowance < \ChunkLease\) (equation 4.4).
\(\RenewalJitterAllowance\) Renewal-Jitter Allowance \(\text{ms}\) Allowance for variation in the interval between successful admissions; chosen, then checked against measured renewal intervals.
\(\PermissionLoopPeriod\) Permission-Loop Period \(\text{ms}\) Period of the permission loop, which enters \(\PrebrakeDelay\) because a hazard may wait up to one period before it is checked.
\(\CommandTransportTime\) Command Transport Time \(\text{ms}\) Fieldbus delivery time from the permission path to the drive, which enters \(\PrebrakeDelay\).
\(\ActuatorResponseTime\) Actuator Response Time \(\text{ms}\) Time from a delivered stop command to retarding force, including gate-drive and current rise, which enters \(\PrebrakeDelay\).
\(\DetectionInterval\) Detection Interval \(\text{ms}\) Recognition or expiry-detection time after the decision-start epoch, not already charged to \(\ObservationAge\); a term of the lease-expiry and hazard-detection forms of \(\PrebrakeDelay\).
\(\FallbackHold\) Fallback Hold \(\text{ms}\) Optional, separately authorized hold or fault-confirmation interval before the stop command reaches the drive; zero when the stop path has none.
\(\ProposerInferenceTime\) Proposer Inference Time \(\text{ms}\) Time for one complete evaluation of the learned proposer (prefill and decode); spent above the proposal boundary and outside the permission loop’s period.
\(\DispatchAdmissionInterval\) Dispatch-to-Admission Interval \(\text{ms}\) Elapsed time from perception dispatch to the admission of the candidate it conditions; evidence keeps aging through it. Not a term of \(\PrebrakeDelay\); its travel \(\SpeedAtDetection\,\DispatchAdmissionInterval\) is charged against the stopping budget’s timing allowance.
\(\TakeoverResponseTime, \DisengagedTakeoverResponseTime\) Takeover Response Time \(\text{ms}, \text{s}\) Human response plus console transport for an in-loop (\(\TakeoverResponseTime\)) and an out-of-loop (\(\DisengagedTakeoverResponseTime\)) takeover; it adds to \(\PrebrakeDelay\) while the machine waits for the person.
\(\CompletionDeadline\) Deadline \(\text{ms}, \text{s}\) A duration budget a computation or handover must meet (the series symbol); qualifiers name the instance, as in \(\FallbackDeadline\), the latest permissible wait before the fallback must begin. A deadline stated as a clock instant is lowercase, \(\DeadlineInstant\) or \(\Glyph{t_{\text{deadline,stop}}}\).
\(\HandoverTimeout\) Handover Timeout \(\text{s}\) Timer an arbiter sets for an unacknowledged handover, \(\HandoverTimeout = \max(0, \min(\HandoverTimeoutCeiling, \FallbackDeadline))\), where \(\HandoverTimeoutCeiling\) is the protocol ceiling.
\(\CommitmentInstant\) Commitment Instant \(\text{s}\) Instant after which a receding-horizon plan’s prefix is committed and can no longer be replaced by a new proposal.
\(\HazardOnset\) Hazard Onset \(\text{s}\) Instant at which a hazard first becomes physically present, the reference from which detection and stopping intervals are measured.

Leases

A lease is authority that lapses unless it is renewed. The book uses six kinds:

Lease Symbol Distinction
Execution lease remaining \(\RemainingExecutionLease\) Any authority an admitted command source holds to keep driving the actuators without renewal; it begins at admission, and its expiry invokes the state-matched fallback.
Chunk lease \(\ChunkLease\) The execution lease of the autonomous chunk policy, renewed by each admitted chunk; it enters \(\PrebrakeDelay\).
Manual lease \(\ManualLease\) The execution lease of a human operator in manual mode, renewed by authenticated heartbeats; it replaces the chunk lease in \(\PrebrakeDelay\) while the operator drives.
Administrative lease \(\IntentAdministrativeLease\) The host-silence bound inside an intent lease: how long the application processor may hold a goal without renewing it.
Intent lease expiry \(\IntentLeaseExpiry\) The record that grants a grounded target and effort ceilings, never a path; it expires at the earliest of its evidence horizon, its administrative lease, and its stopping deadline.
Hardware lease (mechanism) The countdown timer on the safety microcontroller that enforces any of these expiries even when the process that issued the authority has hung.

Single-device execution time

In a serial model without overlap, one evaluation of a learned proposer on the robot’s onboard accelerator takes a time that splits into three terms: moving bytes through memory, executing operations, and a fixed overhead. The chunk-cadence and proposer-inference relationships below reuse these symbols, with a subscript naming the instance (\(\SustainedMemoryBandwidth\), \(\PrefillOperations\)):

\[\Time = \underbrace{\frac{\DataVolume}{\Bandwidth}}_{\text{Memory Time}} + \underbrace{\frac{\Operations}{\PeakRate \cdot \EfficiencyHw}}_{\text{Compute Time}} + \underbrace{\Latency}_{\text{Latency Overhead}}\]

Symbol Definition Unit Notes
\(\Time\) Execution Time seconds Total execution time of one evaluation; bare \(\Glyph{T}\) is always time.
\(\Bandwidth\) Bandwidth bytes/s Sustained bytes per second across a named path; never bare \(\Glyph{B}\), which is batch size \(\BatchSize\).
\(\DataVolume\) Data Volume bytes Bytes moved through memory in one evaluation; never bare \(\Glyph{D}\), which is dataset size.
\(\EfficiencyHw\) Efficiency — Fraction of the peak rate an evaluation achieves, \(0 \le \EfficiencyHw \le 1\); never bare \(\Glyph{\eta}\), which is the learning rate.
\(\Latency\) Latency seconds Fixed overhead per evaluation (kernel launch, transfer setup); the subscript keeps it distinct from the loss \(\LossFunction\).
\(\Operations\) Operations FLOPs Floating-point operations one evaluation executes; a subscript names the phase (\(\PrefillOperations\)). Asymptotic cost is \(\mathcal{O}(\cdot)\).
\(\PeakRate\) Peak Rate FLOP/s Peak arithmetic rate of the processor at the stated precision; never bare \(\Glyph{P}\), which is parameter count.

Action-chunk cadence and proposer inference

Large neural policies that stream their weights from memory may exceed a kilohertz control period. Action chunking predicts an action trajectory of horizon \(\ActionChunkHorizon\) at a lower cognitive frequency \(\Glyph{f_{\text{policy}}}\), while the chunk’s waypoints are consumed at \(\ActionExecutionRate\). In the serial model with one weight-streaming pass per chunk and no additional fixed overhead, memory streaming must fit inside the chunk execution window after reserving the compute term \(\Glyph{T_{\text{compute}}}\) of the single-device decomposition:

\[\ParameterStreamingTime = \frac{\WeightMemory}{\SustainedMemoryBandwidth} \le \frac{\ActionChunkHorizon}{\ActionExecutionRate} - \Glyph{T_{\text{compute}}}\]

A decoder that streams its nonresident weights once per emitted token spends, for \(\EmittedTokenCount\) emitted tokens, the inference time

\[\ProposerInferenceTime \approx \max\!\left(\frac{\PrefillOperations}{\EfficiencyHw\,\PeakRate},\ \frac{\WeightMemory}{\SustainedMemoryBandwidth}\right) + (\EmittedTokenCount - 1)\,\frac{\WeightMemory}{\SustainedMemoryBandwidth}\]

which caps the fresh-evaluation rate at \(\ProposerEvaluationRate \le \SustainedMemoryBandwidth/\WeightMemory\) when every evaluation streams the weights. The symbols are those of the single-device decomposition: \(\PrefillOperations\) counts the prefill’s operations, \(\PeakRate\) is the processor’s peak rate at the activation precision, \(\EfficiencyHw\) is the fraction of it the prefill achieves, and \(\ArithmeticIntensity\) is the arithmetic intensity that decides which term binds.

Symbol Definition Unit Notes
\(\ActionChunkHorizon\) Horizon Time steps Number of contiguous future action steps generated in a single policy forward pass (\(\ActionChunkHorizon=16\) on the mobile manipulator; 16 to 64 steps in the literature).
\(\ActionDimension\) Action Dimension Integer Number of components of one action \(\CognitivePolicyAction_{t}\); a chunk \(\Glyph{\mathbf{A}_{t:t+H}}\) has \(\ActionChunkHorizon \times \ActionDimension\) entries.
\(\SustainedMemoryBandwidth\) Sustained Memory Bandwidth bytes/s Achievable memory throughput of the SoC or accelerator memory subsystem (e.g., LPDDR5, HBM); the core’s \(\Bandwidth\) at the memory interface.
\(\ParameterStreamingTime\) Parameter Streaming Time \(\text{ms}\) Time required to stream non-resident model weights into execution registers.
\(\ActionExecutionRate\) Action Execution Rate \(\text{Hz}\) Temporal consumption rate of chunked waypoints along the planned trajectory (\(100\text{ Hz}\) to \(1\text{ kHz}\)).
\(\EmittedTokenCount\) Emitted Token Count tokens Output tokens an autoregressive decoder emits per evaluation; the core’s sequence length \(\SequenceLength\) for the decoded part. Never \(\Glyph{D}\), which is dataset size.

Stopping, braking, and safe-set invariants

For the stopping-based safety envelope, the machine must retain enough clearance to arrest its approach before crossing the protected boundary. The relationship below specializes the resident stop to one-dimensional motion starting and ending at zero acceleration, with symmetric jerk ramps and a constant-deceleration segment. The stopping distance combines travel during the pre-brake delay with braking travel; the delay-budget inequality adds the spatial margins:

\[\StoppingDistance(\SpeedAtDetection) = \SpeedAtDetection\,\PrebrakeDelay + \ResidentStopDistance(\SpeedAtDetection), \qquad \ResidentStopDistance(\SpeedAtDetection) = \frac{\SpeedAtDetection^2}{2\CredibleBrakingDeceleration} + \frac{\SpeedAtDetection\,\CredibleBrakingDeceleration}{2\JerkLimit} \quad (\SpeedAtDetection \ge \CredibleBrakingDeceleration^2/\JerkLimit)\]

Symbol Definition Unit Notes
\(\StoppingDistance\) Stopping Distance \(\text{m}\) Distance required to bring the plant to rest from speed \(\SpeedAtDetection\).
\(\StoppingDuration\) Stopping Duration \(\text{s}\) Elapsed time from braking onset to standstill in the protective-separation calculation; distinct from controller sampling period.
\(\ClearDistance\) Clear Distance \(\text{m}\) Clear distance from the machine to the boundary it must not cross; the subscript keeps it distinct from dataset size \(\Glyph{D}\).
\(\SpeedAtDetection\) Speed at Detection \(\text{m/s}\) Speed of the machine at the moment the hazard is detected; in stopping, pre-brake, and impact algebra, the scalar travel or approach speed \(\SpeedAtDetection\).
\(\SpeedBound\) Speed Bound \(\text{m/s}\) Largest speed the machine, a tracked object, or an approaching body is credited with in a budget; the bounding speed in \(\TelemetryFreshnessBound \le \PositionErrorCeiling/\SpeedBound\).
\(\CredibleBrakingDeceleration\) Credible Deceleration \(\text{m/s}^2\) Validated low tail of the deceleration the machine achieves under its stated conditions, not the datasheet peak.
\(\ProfileAccelerationBound\) Profile Acceleration Bound \(\text{m/s}^2\) Largest acceleration or deceleration a planned or interpolated profile may command; for the base’s resident stop it is set to \(\CredibleBrakingDeceleration\).
\(\NetBrakingDeceleration\) Net Braking Deceleration \(\text{m/s}^2, \text{rad/s}^2\) Linear or angular deceleration left after a gravity or load bias torque \(\Glyph{\tau_{\text{bias}}}\) is subtracted from the actuator’s braking capacity, in the direction of travel.
\(\ResidentStopDistance(\SpeedAtDetection)\) Resident Stop Distance \(\text{m}\) Travel of the resident stop from speed \(\SpeedAtDetection\): \(\SpeedAtDetection^2/(2\CredibleBrakingDeceleration)\) for constant-deceleration braking; for the jerk-limited stop of equation 11.17, that term plus the jerk ramps’ \(\SpeedAtDetection\,\CredibleBrakingDeceleration/(2\JerkLimit)\) once \(\SpeedAtDetection \ge \CredibleBrakingDeceleration^2/\JerkLimit\).
\(\LocalizationBound\) Localization Bound \(\text{m}\) Bound on localization and state-estimation error.
\(\ProtectiveClearanceMargin\) Protective Clearance \(\text{m}\) Fixed, nonzero protective clearance between the machine envelope and the boundary.
\(\TrackingErrorBound\) Tracking-Error Bound \(\text{m}\) Bound on the deviation between the commanded and the executed trajectory, stated by the enforcement record.
\(\HumanWalkingSpeedBound\) Human Walking-Speed Bound \(\text{m/s}\) Walking-speed bound for a person approaching the machine, used in the form its safety standard states.
\(\JerkLimit\) Jerk Limit \(\text{m/s}^3\) Maximum rate of change of deceleration; sets the jerk ramps of the resident stop in \(\ResidentStopDistance(\SpeedAtDetection)\) (equation 11.17) and bounds the bumpless-transfer window \(\BlendingTransitionWindow\).

Zero-order hold and phase margin

At crossover frequencies well below the sampling frequency, holding discrete control commands constant over sampling period \(\SamplingPeriod = 1/\SamplingRate\) contributes approximately half a sample period of delay. With fixed modeled enforcer and fieldbus delays, the relationship below estimates phase-margin erosion; its phase-lag term is in radians, so all phase margins must use the same angular unit before subtraction:

\[\ZohTransportDelay = \frac{\SamplingPeriod}{2}, \qquad \DelayPhaseLag = \CrossoverFrequency \left( \frac{\SamplingPeriod}{2} + \EnforcerComputeTime + \CommandTransportTime \right), \qquad \Glyph{\text{PM}_{\text{net}}} = \text{PM}_{\text{plant}} - \DelayPhaseLag > 0\]

Symbol Definition Unit Notes
\(\ZohTransportDelay\) ZOH Transport Delay \(\text{s}\) Equivalent continuous delay introduced by discrete Zero-Order Hold reconstruction (\(\SamplingPeriod / 2\)).
\(\SamplingPeriod, \SamplingRate\) Sampling Period and Rate \(\text{s}, \text{Hz}\) Discrete controller execution period and frequency (\(\SamplingRate = 1/\SamplingPeriod\)).
\(\EnforcerComputeTime\) Enforcer Compute Time \(\text{s}\) Time the enforcer takes to evaluate its constraints within one control period; with \(\CommandTransportTime\) it adds to the ZOH delay.
\(\PureTransportDelay\) Pure Transport Delay \(\text{s}\) A fixed delay in a feedback or communication channel, contributing phase lag \(\AngularFrequency \PureTransportDelay\) at frequency \(\AngularFrequency\).
\(\CrossoverFrequency\) Open-Loop Crossover Frequency \(\text{rad/s}\) Frequency at which open-loop gain \(\lvert \OpenLoopTransferFunction(\ImaginaryUnit\CrossoverFrequency) \rvert = 1\) (\(0\text{ dB}\)).
\(\DelayPhaseLag\) Phase Lag Degradation \(\text{rad}\) / \(\text{deg}\) Phase erosion at crossover (\(\DelayPhaseLag \approx 28.65^\circ \cdot (\CrossoverFrequency / \SamplingRate)\) from the hold alone) depleting closed-loop stability margin.

Actuator electrical and thermal limits

Actuators are constrained by copper winding heating, DC bus rail droop, and magnetic saturation. Sustainable torque output is governed by the coupled electromechanical balance, written per joint with scalar torque:

\[\DcBusVoltage = \PhaseInductance \frac{d\TorqueProducingCurrent}{d\TimeInstant} + \ElectricalResistance(\WindingTemperature)\, \TorqueProducingCurrent + \MotorBackEmfConstant \RotorAngularSpeed, \qquad \StatorThermalCapacitance \frac{d\WindingTemperature}{d\TimeInstant} = \TorqueProducingCurrent^2 \ElectricalResistance(\WindingTemperature) - \frac{\WindingTemperature - \AmbientTemperature}{\ThermalResistance}, \qquad \CommandedJointTorque = \GearboxTransmissionEfficiency \GearReductionRatio \MotorTorqueConstant \TorqueProducingCurrent\]

Symbol Definition Unit Notes
\(\MotorTorqueConstant, \MotorBackEmfConstant\) Torque and Back-EMF Constants \(\text{N}\cdot\text{m/A}, \text{V}\cdot\text{s/rad}\) Motor electromechanical coupling constants linking winding current to rotor shaft torque and rotor speed to back-EMF voltage (\(\MotorTorqueConstant \approx \MotorBackEmfConstant\) in SI units).
\(\TorqueProducingCurrent\) Torque-Producing Current \(\text{A}\) Quadrature-axis current of a field-oriented drive, \(\MotorTorque = \MotorTorqueConstant \TorqueProducingCurrent\). Bare \(\Glyph{I}\) is never a current, because the series core reserves it for arithmetic intensity.
\(\MotorPhaseRmsCurrent\) Motor Phase RMS Current \(\text{A}\) RMS phase current driving the stator windings of a brushless PM motor; an uppercase \(\Glyph{I}\) with a subscript names an RMS value or rating.
\(\InstantaneousPhaseCurrent\) Instantaneous Phase Current \(\text{A}\) Signed instantaneous electrical phase current, positive from inverter node into winding; distinct from RMS phase current and torque-producing dq current.
\(\ContinuousCurrent, \PeakCurrent\) Continuous / Peak Current \(\text{A}\) RMS current ratings bounded by winding insulation class and inverter MOSFET dissipation.
\(\PhaseInductance, \PhaseResistance\) Phase Inductance and Resistance \(\text{H}, \Glyph{\Omega}\) Stator winding inductance and DC resistance; they set \(\ElectricalTimeConstant = \PhaseInductance/\PhaseResistance\).
\(\ElectricalResistance(\WindingTemperature)\) Temperature-Dependent Resistance \(\Glyph{\Omega}\) Stator copper resistance at winding temperature \(\WindingTemperature\): \(\ElectricalResistance(\WindingTemperature) = \ReferenceWindingResistance [1 + \CopperTemperatureCoefficient(\WindingTemperature - \CopperReferenceTemperature)]\) with \(\CopperTemperatureCoefficient = 0.00393\text{ K}^{-1}\).
\(\ThermalResistance\) Thermal Resistance \(\text{K/W}\) Lumped thermal resistance from stator windings to the ambient environment; path segments carry a second subscript (\(\JunctionAmbientThermalResistance\), \(\JunctionCaseThermalResistance\), \(\WindingCoreThermalResistance\), \(\CoreAmbientThermalResistance\)) and add in series.
\(\StatorThermalCapacitance\) Stator Thermal Capacitance \(\text{J/K}\) Lumped heat capacity of the motor core, windings, and potting compound.
\(\ElectricalTimeConstant\) Electrical Time Constant \(\text{s}\) Winding current rise time constant \(\ElectricalTimeConstant = \PhaseInductance/\PhaseResistance\); current reaches \(1 - e^{-1}\) of its target in \(\ElectricalTimeConstant\).
\(\ThermalTimeConstant\) Thermal Time Constant \(\text{s}\) Actuator heating characteristic time constant (\(\ThermalTimeConstant = \ThermalResistance \StatorThermalCapacitance\), typically \(60\text{--}300\text{ s}\)); a single stage takes a second subscript (\(\CopperThermalTimeConstant\), \(\CaseThermalTimeConstant\)).
\(\WindingTemperature, \AmbientTemperature\) Winding and Ambient Temperature \(\text{K}\) or \(^\circ\text{C}\) The thermal model’s state and boundary temperatures; the other temperatures follow the subscript rule of the disambiguation table (\(\JunctionTemperature\), \(\CaseTemperature\), \(\WindingTemperatureLimit\), \(\EquilibriumWindingTemperature\), \(\CopperReferenceTemperature\)).
\(\JouleanTemperatureRise\) Joulean Temperature Rise \(\text{K}\) Thermal rise above ambient; \(\JouleanTemperatureRise = \WindingLossPower \ThermalResistance\) gives the steady-state rise for constant loss power in the lumped model. Transient rise follows the thermal balance; never a time interval.
\(\ActuatorDutyCycle\) Actuator Duty Cycle \([0, 1]\) Ratio of active pulse duration to cycle period (\(\ActuatorOnTime / (\ActuatorOnTime + \ActuatorOffTime)\)) preventing thermal runaway.

Shielded hazards and the exposure bound

Catastrophic failure rates in embodied systems cannot be certified through stochastic testing alone. For a defined hazard class \(\HazardClassIndex\), verified interception coverage and independently bounded residual faults constrain the system’s hazard rate. Under a constant-rate Poisson exposure model, zero-failure exposure follows the Rule of Three:

\[\SystemHazardRate \le \HazardousProposalRate (1 - \ShieldInterceptionCoverage) + \HardwareCommonCauseRate \le \TargetHazardRate, \qquad \FailureObservationInterval \ge \frac{-\ln \HypothesisTestSignificanceLevel}{\TargetHazardRate}\]

Here \(\FailureObservationInterval\) is the zero-incident operating exposure, in hours. For independent trials with a common success probability, per-trial campaigns use the same bound in probability form, \(\ZeroFailureTrialCount \ge \ln\HypothesisTestSignificanceLevel/\ln \TargetTrialSuccessProbability \approx 3/(1 - \TargetTrialSuccessProbability)\).

Symbol Definition Unit Notes
\(\SystemHazardRate\) System Hazard Rate \(\text{h}^{-1}\) Certified dangerous failure rate of the integrated cyber-physical machine for hazard class \(\HazardClassIndex\).
\(\HazardousProposalRate\) Hazardous Proposal Rate \(\text{h}^{-1}\) Arrival rate of unshielded hazardous action proposals the policy emits before the permission layer, measured against a counterfactual oracle.
\(\ShieldInterceptionCoverage\) Shield Interception Coverage Dimensionless Verified conditional probability that the shield intercepts a hazardous proposal of class \(\HazardClassIndex\), evaluated over the states in which the policy errs.
\(\HardwareCommonCauseRate\) Hardware and Common-Cause Rate \(\text{h}^{-1}\) Independently bounded hardware-fault and common-cause hazard rate the shield cannot intercept; the floor \(\SystemHazardRate\) reaches when \(\ShieldInterceptionCoverage = 1\).
\(\TargetHazardRate\) Target Hazard Rate \(\text{h}^{-1}\) Tolerable dangerous failure rate assigned to the safety function by risk assessment, in the band its governing standard defines.
\(\DiagnosticCoverage\) Diagnostic Coverage Dimensionless Rate-weighted fraction of hardware faults whose hazardous effect is mitigated, \(\UnmitigatedHardwareFaultRate = \RawHardwareFaultRate(1 - \DiagnosticCoverage)\); the hardware counterpart of \(\ShieldInterceptionCoverage\), which covers policy proposals.
\(\TrialFailureProbability, \TargetTrialSuccessProbability\) Per-Trial Probabilities Dimensionless Per-trial failure probability, and the per-trial success probability a campaign must demonstrate; trial counts follow \(\ZeroFailureTrialCount \ge \ln\HypothesisTestSignificanceLevel/\ln \TargetTrialSuccessProbability\).
\(\HypothesisTestSignificanceLevel\) Statistical Significance Level Dimensionless Type I error threshold (\(\HypothesisTestSignificanceLevel = 1 - \text{CL}\), e.g., \(\HypothesisTestSignificanceLevel=0.05\) for 95% confidence where \(-\ln(0.05) \approx 3.0\)).
\(\StatisticalFalseNegativeRate\) Type II Error Allowance Dimensionless Probability of rejecting an acceptable candidate (\(1-\StatisticalFalseNegativeRate\) is the power); with \(\HypothesisTestSignificanceLevel\) it sets the SPRT boundaries \(\SprtUpperBoundary\) and \(\SprtLowerBoundary\). Diffusion schedules use indexed \(\Glyph{\beta_s}\); a KL weight \(\Glyph{\beta}\) stays local to its objective.
\(\ZeroFailureTrialCount\) Zero-Failure Trial Count Trials Required number of incident-free trials to substantiate \(\TargetTrialSuccessProbability\); for exposure measured in hours, the bound is on \(\FailureObservationInterval\) instead.
\(\SequentialLogLikelihoodRatio\) Sequential Log-Likelihood Ratio Dimensionless After \(\ObservedTrialFailureCount\) failures in \(\SequentialTrialCount\) trials, \(\SequentialLogLikelihoodRatio = \ObservedTrialFailureCount\ln(\HypothesizedTrialFailureProbability_1/\HypothesizedTrialFailureProbability_0) + (\SequentialTrialCount-\ObservedTrialFailureCount)\ln\frac{1-\HypothesizedTrialFailureProbability_1}{1-\HypothesizedTrialFailureProbability_0}\) for per-trial failure probabilities \(\HypothesizedTrialFailureProbability_0\) (unacceptable) and \(\HypothesizedTrialFailureProbability_1\) (acceptable); SPRT stops when it leaves \((\SprtLowerBoundary, \SprtUpperBoundary)\).
\(\SprtUpperBoundary, \SprtLowerBoundary\) SPRT Boundaries Dimensionless Upper and lower stopping thresholds on \(\SequentialLogLikelihoodRatio\), \(\SprtUpperBoundary \approx \ln((1-\StatisticalFalseNegativeRate)/\HypothesisTestSignificanceLevel)\) and \(\SprtLowerBoundary \approx \ln(\StatisticalFalseNegativeRate/(1-\HypothesisTestSignificanceLevel))\); never bare \(\Glyph{A}\) and \(\Glyph{B}\).
\(\AssuranceClaim\) Assurance Claim Proposition Top-level safety claim of an assurance argument. Under an independent-premise probability model, its confidence \(\Pr(\AssuranceClaim)\) is the product of the premise confidences for a conjunctive argument, and one minus the product of the legs’ failure probabilities for a multi-legged one.

The embodied energy corollary

Untethered mobile manipulators and autonomous robots operate under strict battery energy reserves and peak thermal dissipation ceilings. Total mission energy (distinct from the ML workload’s \(\Glyph{E_{\text{total}}}\)) splits across mechanical actuation work, edge neural computation, and auxiliary sensor payloads. The compute energy expended by edge inference system-on-chip (SoC) devices decomposes into data movement and arithmetic operations according to the core energy corollary:

\[\MissionEnergy = \Glyph{E_{\text{act}}} + \Glyph{E_{\text{total}}} + \Glyph{E_{\text{aux}}}, \qquad \Glyph{E_{\text{total}}} \approx \DataVolume \times \Glyph{E_{\text{move}}} + \Operations \times \Glyph{E_{\text{compute}}}\]

For constant loss power in the single-node thermal model, resistive heating in stator windings \(\WindingLossPower \approx \sum \TorqueProducingCurrent^2 \ElectricalResistance(\WindingTemperature)\) sets the steady-state rise \(\JouleanTemperatureRise = \WindingLossPower \ThermalResistance\). Transient heating during shorter acceleration or contact bursts follows the coupled first-order thermal balance above.

Plant State, Actuation, and Kinematics

The symbols below describe the mechanical configuration, velocities, forces, and inertia of articulated and mobile mechanisms. Units for inertia and generalized loads specialize to revolute coordinates; mixed revolute–prismatic plants require entrywise units consistent with their generalized coordinates.

Symbol Definition Unit Notes
\(\PhysicalWorldState_{t} \in \WorldStateSpace\) Physical World State Mixed Ground-truth continuous state of the machine, environment, and physical contacts at time \(\TimeInstant\), and the state of the decision process in the learning chapters; italic, because it mixes types.
\(\MechanicalPlantState\) Mechanical Plant State Mixed: \(\text{rad}\) or \(\text{m}\) for positions; \(\text{rad/s}\) or \(\text{m/s}\) for velocities Generalized state vector \(\MechanicalPlantState = [\GeneralizedCoordinates^\top, \GeneralizedVelocities^\top]^\top \in \mathbb{R}^{2\GeneralizedCoordinateCount}\) containing positions and velocities.
\(\GeneralizedCoordinates \in \mathbb{R}^\GeneralizedCoordinateCount\) Generalized Coordinates \(\text{rad}\) or \(\text{m}\) Degrees of freedom describing mechanical articulation (joint angles and prismatic link extensions); entries \(\JointCoordinate_i\).
\(\GeneralizedVelocities \in \mathbb{R}^\GeneralizedCoordinateCount\) Generalized Velocities \(\text{rad/s}\) or \(\text{m/s}\) First time derivative of generalized coordinates.
\(\GeneralizedAccelerations \in \mathbb{R}^\GeneralizedCoordinateCount\) Generalized Accelerations \(\text{rad/s}^2\) or \(\text{m/s}^2\) Second time derivative of generalized coordinates.
\(\JointTorque \in \mathbb{R}^\GeneralizedCoordinateCount\) Actuator Torque Vector \(\text{N}\cdot\text{m}\) or \(\text{N}\) Generalized forces and torques delivered by actuators at mechanical joints; a subscript names a source, as in the contact torque \(\Glyph{\JointTorque_c}\).
\(\CommandedJointTorque\) Commanded Joint Torque \(\text{N}\cdot\text{m}\) Scalar torque commanded at one joint in single-joint derivations such as the kinetic barrier; bounds and disturbances keep a subscript (\(\JointTorqueLimit\), \(\Glyph{\tau_{\text{bias}}}\)).
\(\GeneralizedInertiaMatrix(\GeneralizedCoordinates)\) Generalized Inertia Matrix \(\text{kg}\cdot\text{m}^2\) Symmetric positive-definite mass/inertia matrix of the articulated multibody plant.
\(\CoriolisCentrifugalMatrix(\GeneralizedCoordinates, \GeneralizedVelocities)\) Coriolis / Centrifugal Matrix \(\text{N}\cdot\text{m}\cdot\text{s/rad}\) Matrix satisfying \(\Glyph{\dot{\mathbf{M}}} - 2\CoriolisCentrifugalMatrix\) skew-symmetry (passivity property).
\(\GravitationalTorqueVector(\GeneralizedCoordinates)\) Gravitational Torque Vector \(\text{N}\cdot\text{m}\) Generalized gravitational load acting on links as a function of configuration; a single joint’s share is \(\Glyph{\tau_g}\).
\(\ManipulatorJacobian(\GeneralizedCoordinates)\) Manipulator Jacobian Mixed Linear mapping \(\Glyph{\mathbf{v}_e} = \ManipulatorJacobian(\GeneralizedCoordinates)\GeneralizedVelocities\) from joint rates to operational task-space velocity.
\(\YoshikawaManipulability(\GeneralizedCoordinates)\) Yoshikawa Manipulability Depends on task coordinates and scaling Singularity proximity metric \(\YoshikawaManipulability(\GeneralizedCoordinates) = \sqrt{\det(\ManipulatorJacobian\ManipulatorJacobian^\top)}\) vanishing at kinematic singular configurations.
\(\BackEmfVoltage\) Back-EMF Voltage \(\text{V}\) Opposing voltage \(\BackEmfVoltage = \MotorBackEmfConstant \RotorAngularSpeed\) developed across stator coils by rotor rotation.
\(\RotorInertia, \LoadInertia\) Rotor and Load Inertia \(\text{kg}\cdot\text{m}^2\) Moment of inertia of motor rotor shaft and coupled mechanical load.
\(\ReflectedRotorInertia\) Reflected Rotor Inertia \(\text{kg}\cdot\text{m}^2\) Effective inertia \(\ReflectedRotorInertia = \GearReductionRatio^2 \RotorInertia\) amplified quadratically by gearbox ratio.
\(\EffectiveJointInertia\) Effective Joint Inertia \(\text{kg}\cdot\text{m}^2\) Total inertia seen at one joint, link and payload plus \(\ReflectedRotorInertia\); the inertia in single-joint torque and acceleration budgets.
\(\GearReductionRatio\) Gear Reduction Ratio Dimensionless Transmission reduction ratio (\(\GearReductionRatio = \RotorAngularSpeed/\JointAngularSpeed_j \gg 1\)) between motor rotor and joint output shaft; never bare \(\Glyph{N}\).
\(\InertiaMatchingRatio\) Optimal Inertia-Matching Ratio Dimensionless Ratio \(\InertiaMatchingRatio = \sqrt{\LoadInertia / \RotorInertia}\) maximizing load angular acceleration under peak torque.
\(\GearboxTransmissionEfficiency\) Transmission Efficiency Dimensionless Mechanical power transmission efficiency (\(0 < \GearboxTransmissionEfficiency \le 1\)); never bare \(\LearningRate\), which is the learning rate.
\(\PeakTorque, \HoldingTorque\) Continuous / Peak / Holding Torque \(\text{N}\cdot\text{m}\) Thermally continuous holding torque \(\HoldingTorque\) and transient peak acceleration torque.
\(\Mass\) Mass \(\text{kg}\) Physical body or payload mass, lowercase italic; effective and reduced impact masses carry subscripts (\(\EffectiveImpactMass\), \(\ReducedImpactMass\)).
\(\Gravity\) Gravitational Acceleration \(\text{m/s}^2\) Scalar magnitude of gravitational acceleration; the vector \(\GravityVector\) and the configuration-dependent torque \(\GravitationalTorqueVector(\GeneralizedCoordinates)\) are bold.
\(\LinearMomentum\) Linear Momentum \(\text{kg}\cdot\text{m/s}\) Scalar momentum \(\Mass \BodySpeed\) of a moving body; distinct from the bold position \(\CartesianPosition\) and from a distribution \(\ProbabilityFunction(\cdot)\).
\(\WheelRadius\) Wheel Radius \(\text{m}\) Rolling radius of a driven wheel, which converts wheel torque to floor force and speed to wheel rate (\(\Glyph{\omega} = \BodySpeed/\WheelRadius\)); the barrier relative degree \(\BarrierRelativeDegree\) appears only in barrier analysis.
\(\AngularSpeed\) Angular Speed \(\text{rad/s}\) Magnitude of a rigid body’s angular velocity \(\AngularVelocity\); not the crossover frequency \(\Glyph{\omega_c}\).
\(\BaseForwardSpeed\) Base Forward Speed \(\text{m/s}\) Forward linear speed of the mobile base along its heading, the speed input of the unicycle model; not the speed at the instant a hazard is detected.
\(\BaseHeadingRate\) Base Heading Rate \(\text{rad/s}\) Rate of change of a mobile base’s heading angle, the turn input of the unicycle model.
\(\BaseLateralVelocity\) Base Lateral Velocity \(\text{m/s}\) Lateral velocity of a mobile base in the plane.
\(\BaseLongitudinalVelocity\) Base Longitudinal Velocity \(\text{m/s}\) Forward velocity of a mobile base in the plane.
\(\BodySpeed\) Body Speed \(\text{m/s}\) Magnitude of the linear velocity of a moving body or machine; the scalar speed, with the vector velocity bold.
\(\CommandedJointTorqueSlew\) Commanded Joint Torque Slew \(\text{N}\cdot\text{m/s}\) Time derivative of the torque at a single joint; for constant effective inertia it equals \(\EffectiveJointInertia\) times the joint jerk.
\(\ContinuousTorque\) Continuous Torque \(\text{N}\cdot\text{m}\) Largest motor torque a winding can sustain indefinitely at its temperature limit under the stated cooling; set by heat rejection, and at the engineering ceiling it gives the installed rating.
\(\DirectAxisCurrent\) Direct-Axis Current \(\text{A}\) Current of a field-oriented drive along the rotor field, which produces no torque and is normally regulated to zero; not the torque-producing quadrature current.
\(\ElectricalAngularSpeed\) Electrical Angular Speed \(\text{rad/s}\) Rotor speed times the pole-pair count; the electrical frequency of the drive, not the mechanical shaft speed.
\(\ElectricalRotorAngle\) Electrical Rotor Angle \(\text{rad}\) Mechanical rotor angle times the pole-pair count, the angle a field-oriented drive uses to rotate between stator and rotor frames.
\(\EncoderAngle\) Encoder Angle \(\text{rad}\) Joint angle an encoder reports; a sample index appears as a subscript, as in \({\EncoderAngle}_{k}\).
\(\ForwardKinematicsMap\) Forward Kinematics Map Function Map from joint positions to the positions of points on the robot’s links in the base frame.
\(\GeneralizedCoordinateCount\) Generalized Coordinate Count Dimensionless Number of generalized coordinates of a mechanism, its degrees of freedom (the joints of a serial arm); the plant state has twice this many entries.
\(\HeadingAngle\) Heading Angle \(\text{rad}\) Heading of a planar mobile base in the unicycle model, the direction its forward speed points.
\(\ConveyorSpeed\) Conveyor Speed \(\text{m/s}\) Speed of the belt that carries a workpiece past the pick station; not the bound on a takeaway conveyor, \(\ConveyorSpeedBound\).
\(\DrivenWheelLoadFraction\) Driven-Wheel Load Fraction Dimensionless Fraction of a vehicle’s total weight carried by its driven wheels; it scales the traction ceiling on braking deceleration.
\(\JointCoordinate\) Joint Coordinate \(\text{rad}\) or \(\text{m}\) One entry of the generalized coordinates \(\GeneralizedCoordinates\), a single joint’s angle or extension.
\(\JointPosition\) Joint Position \(\text{rad}\) or \(\text{m}\) Position of a single joint along its axis, an angle for a revolute joint and a length for a prismatic one; its time derivatives are \(\JointVelocity\) and \(\JointAcceleration\).
\(\JointAngle\) Joint Angle \(\text{rad}\) Angle of one revolute joint, a scalar; the joint-space vector is \(\GeneralizedCoordinates\).
\(\JointVelocity\) Joint Velocity \(\text{rad/s}\) or \(\text{m/s}\) Velocity of one joint, a scalar; the vector of all joints is \(\GeneralizedVelocities\).
\(\JointAngularSpeed\) Joint Angular Speed \(\text{rad/s}\) Angular speed of a single arm joint; a subscript zero marks the initial value.
\(\JointAcceleration\) Joint Acceleration \(\text{rad/s}^2\) or \(\text{m/s}^2\) Acceleration of one joint, a scalar; the vector of all joints is \(\GeneralizedAccelerations\).
\(\JointJerk\) Joint Jerk \(\text{rad/s}^3\) Jerk of one joint’s trajectory, the third time derivative of its position; a scalar for one joint.
\(\JointCount\) Joint Count Dimensionless Number of joints of a manipulator, the length of its joint-position vector; it shares its glyph with the zero-failure trial count of the validation chapters.
\(\JointIndex\) Joint Index Index Position of one joint axis of a manipulator, from one to the number of joints.
\(\MotorTorque\) Motor Torque \(\text{N}\cdot\text{m}\) Torque a motor produces at its shaft before the transmission, the torque constant times the torque-producing current; the joint output is \(\JointOutputTorque\).
\(\JointOutputTorque\) Joint Output Torque \(\text{N}\cdot\text{m}\) Torque a joint delivers at the transmission output: the transmission efficiency \(\GearboxTransmissionEfficiency\) times the gear ratio \(\GearReductionRatio\) times the motor torque \(\MotorTorque\).
\(\JointFrictionTorque\) Joint Friction Torque \(\text{N}\cdot\text{m}\) Total friction torque of a joint, referred to its output link.
\(\JointTorqueLimit\) Joint Torque Limit \(\text{N}\cdot\text{m}\) Largest torque the actuator of one joint can deliver, indexed by joint; it bounds \(\CommandedJointTorque\) at the same joint and is not the transient burst rating.
\(\JointTorqueUpperLimit\) Joint Torque Upper Limit \(\text{N}\cdot\text{m}\) Per-joint upper bounds on the torque the actuators can deliver, as a vector; the vector counterpart of the scalar \(\JointTorqueLimit\).
\(\JointTorqueSlew\) Joint Torque Slew \(\text{N}\cdot\text{m/s}\) Time derivative of the joint torque vector; it couples to jerk through the derivative of the manipulator equation.
\(\JointVelocityLimit\) Joint Velocity Limit \(\text{rad/s}\) or \(\text{m/s}\) Per-joint bounds on the magnitude of the generalized velocities; a planned trajectory and every admitted command must stay within them.
\(\JointTrackingError\) Joint Tracking Error \(\text{rad}\) or \(\text{m}\) Vector of differences between the desired and the measured joint positions; not the state estimation error, which shares the glyph.
\(\LeverArmLength\) Lever Arm Length \(\text{m}\) Distance from a rotating joint to the point it carries, which converts a joint rotation into tool travel; a scalar length.
\(\JacobianSingularNumber\) Jacobian Singular Value Mixed Singular value of the manipulator Jacobian \(\ManipulatorJacobian\), the tool speed a unit joint rate produces along one singular direction; units follow the Jacobian rows.
\(\InverseKinematicsDamping\) Inverse Kinematics Damping Mixed Damping length that damped-least-squares inverse kinematics adds to bound the joint step near a singularity; it enters squared, and along a singular direction the gain never exceeds one over twice its value. The ridge-regression analogue of weight decay.
\(\PermanentMagnetFluxLinkage\) Permanent-Magnet Flux Linkage \(\text{V}\cdot\text{s}\) Magnetic flux from the rotor magnets that links each stator winding; with the pole-pair count it sets the torque constant.
\(\PlantDynamicsFunction\) Plant Dynamics Function Function Vector field of a plant’s equations of motion, giving the state rate from the state, the control input, and the plant parameters; not the unforced drift part of control-affine dynamics, which shares its glyph.
\(\PlantParameters\) Plant Parameters Record Physical and hardware properties of the machine in one trial or rollout, such as tolerances, wear, payload mass, state of charge, and calibration; not the mass \(\Glyph{m}\) of a body.
\(\PlantStateRate\) Plant State Rate Dimensionless per unit time Time derivative of the mechanical plant state; the decorated form of the plant state, and not the Cartesian velocity of the tool, which shares its glyph.
\(\PlantStateSpace\) Plant State Space Set Set of all states the mechanical plant can occupy, positions together with velocities.
\(\RotorAngularSpeed\) Rotor Angular Speed \(\text{rad/s}\) Angular speed of a motor rotor about its axis; it sets the back-EMF, and on a direct-drive hub motor it equals the wheel’s angular speed.
\(\RotorPolePairCount\) Rotor Pole-Pair Count Dimensionless Number of magnetic pole pairs on a motor rotor; multiplying the mechanical rotor speed by it gives the electrical frequency of the drive.
\(\QuadratureAxisVoltage\) Quadrature-Axis Voltage \(\text{V}\) Voltage the inverter of a field-oriented drive applies along the quadrature axis, the axis that produces torque.
\(\TiltAngle\) Tilt Angle \(\text{rad}\) Angular deflection of a body from vertical; zero when the body is upright.
\(\TorqueRateLimit\) Torque Rate Limit \(\text{N}\cdot\text{m/s}\) Largest rate of change of joint output torque a drive accepts. Divided by the effective joint inertia it bounds joint jerk.
\(\TransmissionTorsionalStiffness\) Transmission Torsional Stiffness \(\text{N}\cdot\text{m/rad}\) Torsional stiffness of the transmission between rotor and output link, the compliance that couples motor to load.
\(\YawRate\) Yaw Rate \(\text{rad/s}\) Angular velocity of a body about its vertical axis, one component of the body angular velocity \(\AngularVelocity\).

Contact Mechanics and Centroidal Dynamics

Contact turns a free-moving body into a constrained one. The symbols here describe the forces a contact can transmit, the friction cone that bounds them, and the centroidal quantities that sum them over the whole robot.

Symbol Definition Unit Notes
\(\ActuationSelectionMatrix \in \mathbb{R}^{\GeneralizedCoordinateCount \times (\GeneralizedCoordinateCount+6)}\) Actuation Selection Matrix Matrix Matrix \([\ZeroMatrix, \IdentityMatrix]\) projecting generalized forces onto actuated joints in floating-base systems.
\(\ContactPointForce_{k} \in \mathbb{R}^3\) Contact Force Vector \(\text{N}\) Physical contact force exerted by environment on contact patch \(\Glyph{k}\) (\(\ContactPointForce_{k} = [\TangentialContactShear^\top, \NormalContactForce]^\top\)); always indexed by the contact.
\(\FingerNormalForce\) Finger normal force N Normal force one finger of a grasp presses against the object at its contact.
\(\FingerTangentialForce\) Finger tangential force N Tangential friction force one finger of a grasp carries at its contact, bounded by the friction cone times \(\FingerNormalForce\).
\({\ContactPointJacobian}_k(\GeneralizedCoordinates)\) Contact Point Jacobian Mixed Mapping joint velocities to contact patch Cartesian linear velocity: \(\Glyph{\dot{\mathbf{p}}}_k = {\ContactPointJacobian}_k(\GeneralizedCoordinates) \GeneralizedVelocities\).
\(\NormalContactForce\) Normal Contact Force \(\text{N}\) Unilateral compressive force (\(\NormalContactForce = \ContactPointForce_{k}^\top \ContactNormal_k \ge 0\)) perpendicular to contact plane.
\(\TangentialContactShear\) Tangential Contact Shear \(\text{N}\) Friction traction force tangential to contact plane (\(\TangentialContactShear = \ContactPointForce_{k} - \NormalContactForce \ContactNormal_k\)).
\(\CoulombFrictionCoefficient\) Coulomb Friction Coefficient Dimensionless Static/kinetic coefficient bounding friction cone: \(\lVert \TangentialContactShear \rVert \le \CoulombFrictionCoefficient \NormalContactForce\).
\(\MassCenterPosition \in \mathbb{R}^3\) Center of Mass (CoM) \(\text{m}\) Spatial coordinates of the total articulated body mass centroid (\(\MassCenterPosition = \frac{1}{\Mass} \sum \Mass_i \CartesianPosition_i\)).
\(\CentroidalLinearMomentum\) Centroidal Linear Momentum \(\text{kg}\cdot\text{m/s}\) Total plant linear momentum (\(\CentroidalLinearMomentum = \Mass \Glyph{\dot{\mathbf{c}}}\)), governed by \(\CentroidalLinearMomentumDerivative = \sum \ContactPointForce_{k} + \Mass \GravityVector\), with gravitational acceleration \(\GravityVector\).
\(\CentroidalAngularMomentum\) Centroidal Angular Momentum \(\text{kg}\cdot\text{m}^2/\text{s}\) Angular momentum about CoM: \(\CentroidalAngularMomentumDerivative = \sum (\CartesianPosition_k - \MassCenterPosition) \times \ContactPointForce_{k} + \ContactPointTorque_k\).
\(\ZeroMomentPoint \in \mathbb{R}^2\) Zero Moment Point (ZMP) \(\text{m}\) Ground-plane point where net horizontal tipping moment vanishes; must reside in \(\text{Conv}(\{\CartesianPosition_k\})\).
\(\RestitutionCoefficient \in [0, 1]\) Coefficient of Restitution Dimensionless Kinematic velocity rebound ratio during non-smooth impact (\(\JointVelocity^+ = -\RestitutionCoefficient \JointVelocity^-\)).
\(\ContactStiffness, \HertzianContactStiffness\) Contact Stiffness \(\text{N/m}\) Elastic stiffness of a structural contact, and of the human body region \(\Glyph{H}\) in an impact (with its effective mass \(\BodyRegionMass\)), in the form ISO/TS 15066 states.
\(\EffectiveImpactMass\) Effective Impact Mass \(\text{kg}\) Apparent normal mass at a specified contact point and configuration. For a single frictionless normal impact, it is the reciprocal of the positive scalar Delassus coefficient formed from the contact normal Jacobian and generalized inertia matrix (Rigid Body Contact Mechanics).
\(\ReducedImpactMass\) Reduced Impact Mass \(\text{kg}\) Two-body reduced mass of a collision, \(\ReducedImpactMass = (1/\EffectiveImpactMass + 1/\BodyRegionMass)^{-1}\), which sets the transferred energy.
\(\UnbrakedImpactForce\) Unbraked Impact Force \(\text{N}\) Transient collision force peak (\(\UnbrakedImpactForce = \SpeedBound \sqrt{\HertzianContactStiffness \ReducedImpactMass}\)) governed by ISO/TS 15066.
\(\ArmStoppingDeceleration\) Arm Stopping Deceleration \(\text{m/s}^2\) Deceleration the arm must achieve after braking onset to stop within the latch force limit.
\(\BodyRegionMass\) Body Region Mass \(\text{kg}\) Effective mass of a human body region, here the hand, in the ISO/TS 15066 body model.
\(\BrakeCommandForce\) Brake Command Contact Force \(\text{N}\) Contact force against the latch at the instant the brake command issues, after the contact loop responds; distinct from a commanded traction force.
\(\BrakingOnsetContactForce\) Braking Onset Contact Force \(\text{N}\) Contact force reached against the latch plate at physical braking onset.
\(\CentroidalAngularMomentumDerivative\) Centroidal Angular Momentum Derivative \(\text{N}\cdot\text{m}\) Time derivative of the centroidal angular momentum, the net external moment about the center of mass: contact-force moments plus the contact torques.
\(\CentroidalLinearMomentumDerivative\) Centroidal Linear Momentum Derivative \(\text{N}\) Time derivative of the centroidal linear momentum, the net external force on the robot: the sum of contact forces plus mass times gravity.
\(\ContactApproachSpeed\) Contact Approach Speed \(\text{m/s}\) Speed at which the robot approaches and strikes a contact surface; contact force rises at stiffness times this speed.
\(\ContactDirectionVelocity\) Contact Direction Velocity \(\text{m/s}\) Velocity of the robot’s contact point along the contact direction.
\(\ContactForce\) Contact Force \(\text{N}\) Force between the robot and the environment at a contact, along the contact direction; the per-point vector is \(\ContactPointForce_{k}\).
\(\ContactLoopResponseTime\) Contact Loop Response Time \(\text{s}\) Time the contact-control loop takes to respond after its tripwire fires.
\(\ContactNormal\) Contact Normal Dimensionless Unit vector normal to the contact plane at one contact, pointing into the robot; indexed by contact.
\(\ContactNormalPosition\) Contact Normal Position \(\text{m}\) Position of a tool or link along the contact normal, whose change from first contact is the contact penetration in the spring contact model.
\(\ContactPointIndex\) Contact Point Index Index Position of one contact point among the contacts of a grasp.
\(\ContactPointOffset\) Contact Point Offset \(\text{m}\) Position of a grasp contact relative to the reference point of the grasped object, the moment arm of its force; indexed by contact.
\(\ContactPointPosition\) Contact Point Position \(\text{m}\) Position of the robot’s contact point along the contact direction.
\(\ContactPointTorque\) Contact Point Torque \(\text{N}\cdot\text{m}\) Moment the environment exerts on the robot at one contact patch, the torque part of the contact wrench; indexed by contact. Not the joint torque vector \(\JointTorque\), which shares the glyph.
\(\ContactTripwireForce\) Contact Tripwire Force \(\text{N}\) Contact force at which the permission path ends an attempt on the machine, set below the structural damage limit.
\(\ContactWrench\) Contact Wrench Varies Force and moment the environment applies at a contact, stacked into one vector; force rows in newtons, moment rows in newton-meters. Privileged in simulation.
\(\ContactWrenchCeiling\) Contact Wrench Ceiling Varies Largest contact force and torque an intent lease permits at the tool, stacked as a wrench; units mix newtons and newton-meters.
\(\DesiredContactPosition\) Desired Contact Position \(\text{m}\) Setpoint position of the robot’s contact point along the contact direction.
\(\DesiredContactVelocity\) Desired Contact Velocity \(\text{m/s}\) Setpoint velocity of the robot’s contact point along the contact direction.
\(\EffectiveSeriesStiffness\) Effective Series Stiffness \(\text{N/m}\) Stiffness of a contact whose two sides deflect in series, the reciprocal of the sum of their compliances; the softer side dominates.
\(\ExternalWrench\) External Wrench Mixed Force and moment the environment applies at the end effector, in newtons for the force and newton-meters for the moments; it maps into joint torques through the transposed manipulator Jacobian.
\(\ExternalContactTorque\) External Contact Torque Vector \(\text{N}\cdot\text{m}\) Joint torques produced by external contact forces acting on the manipulator.
\(\ExternalJointTorque\) External Joint Torque \(\text{N}\cdot\text{m}\) Torque the environment applies at one joint’s output; the scalar counterpart of the external contact torque vector \(\ExternalContactTorque\).
\(\FrictionCoefficientFloor\) Friction Coefficient Floor Dimensionless Lowest friction coefficient a braking or contact premise assumes the surface provides; an unobserved oil spill can invalidate it.
\(\GraspForceCeiling\) Grasp Force Ceiling \(\text{N}\) Largest grasp contact force the object tolerates without crushing or deforming.
\(\GraspForceFloor\) Grasp Force Floor \(\text{N}\) Smallest grasp contact force that holds the object against slip; the grasp must stay between this floor and \(\GraspForceCeiling\).
\(\GraspMap\) Grasp Map Matrix Linear map from contact forces to the wrench they exert on the grasped object; indexed by contact when one contact is meant; not the Kalman gain.
\(\GravityCenterHeight\) Center-of-Gravity Height \(\text{m}\) Height of a vehicle’s center of gravity above the floor; it sets the tip-over ceiling on deceleration.
\(\GravityVector\) Gravity Vector \(\text{m/s}^2\) Gravitational acceleration as a vector pointing down with the magnitude of surface gravity; the scalar is the gravitational acceleration, and it is not the vector of gravitational joint torques.
\(\ImpedanceControlledPosition\) Impedance-Controlled Position \(\text{m}\) Measured position of the point an impedance controller regulates; its deviation from \(\ImpedanceEquilibriumPosition\), times the stiffness, is the spring term.
\(\ImpedanceControlledVelocity\) Impedance-Controlled Velocity \(\text{m/s}\) Measured velocity of the regulated point, the time derivative of \(\ImpedanceControlledPosition\).
\(\ImpedanceEquilibriumPosition\) Impedance Equilibrium Position \(\text{m}\) Virtual rest point of the emulated spring that an impedance controller pulls the controlled point toward.
\(\ImpedanceEquilibriumVelocity\) Impedance Equilibrium Velocity \(\text{m/s}\) Velocity of the virtual equilibrium; its difference from \(\ImpedanceControlledVelocity\), times the damping, is the damper term.
\(\KineticEnergy\) Kinetic Energy \(\text{J}\) Energy a moving body carries, one half its mass times its speed squared (plus rotational terms where stated); not the standard’s limit on energy transferred to a person.
\(\MassCenterHeight\) Center-of-Mass Height \(\text{m}\) Height of a body’s center of mass above its support or contact point; it sets the natural frequency of the linear inverted pendulum, \(\sqrt{\Gravity/\MassCenterHeight}\), and is not a camera-frame coordinate.
\(\PenetrationDepth\) Penetration Depth \(\text{m}\) Depth by which one body penetrates or compresses another at a contact, measured along the contact normal; penalty and compliant contact models compute normal force from it. Shares its glyph with the Huber threshold of the training chapters.
\(\PenetrationRate\) Penetration Rate \(\text{m/s}\) Time rate of change of the penetration depth \(\PenetrationDepth\) at a contact; zero at the instant of maximum compression.
\(\LatchApproachSpeed\) Latch Approach Speed \(\text{m/s}\) Speed at which the arm presses into the cage-door latch plate; the enforcement and release chapters use the same latch scenario.
\(\LatchForceLimit\) Latch Force Limit \(\text{N}\) Structural force limit of the door latch the arm pushes against.
\(\LatchPlateStiffness\) Latch Plate Stiffness \(\text{N/m}\) Spring stiffness of the cage-door latch plate the arm compresses.
\(\LeadingSupportDistance\) Leading Support Distance \(\text{m}\) Horizontal distance from a vehicle’s center of gravity forward to its leading ground support; it shrinks when an arm extends forward.
\(\IntrusionDistance\) Intrusion Distance \(\text{m}\) Distance a body part can intrude past the sensing field before detection; a term of the ISO/TS 15066 protective separation distance.
\(\PersonApproachTravel\) Person Approach Travel \(\text{m}\) Distance a person travels toward the robot during the robot’s reaction and stopping time; a term of the ISO/TS 15066 protective separation distance.
\(\PersonPositionUncertainty\) Person Position Uncertainty \(\text{m}\) Position uncertainty of the person from the sensing system; a term of the ISO/TS 15066 protective separation distance.
\(\KnownComponentStopDistance\) Known-Component Stop Distance \(\text{m}\) Stopping travel that the known components of the stopping budget add up to at a given speed on a dry floor: delay travel, braking, jerk ramps, and fixed overheads; the clear distance minus it is the spare the stopping claim holds in reserve.
\(\PreImpactNormalVelocity\) Pre-Impact Normal Velocity \(\text{m/s}\) Normal contact velocity just before an impact, negative while the bodies approach; the restitution law links it to the post-impact velocity.
\(\PostImpactNormalVelocity\) Post-Impact Normal Velocity \(\text{m/s}\) Normal contact velocity just after an impact, equal to minus the restitution coefficient times the pre-impact normal velocity.
\(\RobotStructuralStiffness\) Robot Structural Stiffness \(\text{N/m}\) Stiffness of the manipulator side of a contact, its pads, linkages, and joint elasticity acting as one series spring; it combines in series with the stiffness of the struck body.
\(\SlipAccelerationBound\) Slip Acceleration Bound \(\text{m/s}^2\) Bound on the residual acceleration of a tracked object slipping relative to its carrier; it widens the evidence horizon of a target estimate.

Geometry, Transformations, and Perception

Every measurement a robot uses arrives in some frame, and every command leaves in another. These symbols name the frames, the rigid transforms between them, and the camera models that project the world onto a sensor.

Symbol Definition Unit Notes
\(\SpecialEuclideanGroup\) Special Euclidean Group Lie Group Group of 3D rigid-body spatial transformations (translations and rotations: \(\mathbb{R}^3 \rtimes \SpecialOrthogonalGroup\)).
\(\SpecialOrthogonalGroup\) Special Orthogonal Group Lie Group Group of \(3 \times 3\) proper orthogonal rotation matrices (\(\DirectionalCosineMatrix^\top \DirectionalCosineMatrix = \IdentityMatrix, \det(\DirectionalCosineMatrix) = +1\)).
\(\WorldReferenceFrame, \BodyReferenceFrame, \Glyph{\mathcal{F}_E}\) Coordinate Reference Frames Reference Fixed World frame (\(\WorldReferenceFrame\)), mobile robot Base/body frame (\(\BodyReferenceFrame\)), and End-effector frame (\(\Glyph{\mathcal{F}_E}\)).
\(\CartesianPosition \in \mathbb{R}^3\) Cartesian 3D Position \(\text{m}\) Position vector \([\Glyph{x}, \Glyph{y}, \Glyph{z}]^\top\) expressing point coordinates in a stated frame.
\(\RigidTransformTranslation \in \mathbb{R}^3\) Rigid-Transform Translation \(\text{m}\) Translation part of a rigid transform between two frames (the offset of one frame’s origin in the other).
\(\DirectionalCosineMatrix \in \SpecialOrthogonalGroup\) Directional Cosine Matrix Matrix \(3 \times 3\) orthonormal matrix parameterizing spatial attitude and orientation.
\(\UnitQuaternion \in \Glyph{\mathbb{H}}\) Unit Quaternion Dimensionless Hypercomplex orientation vector \([\Glyph{w}, \Glyph{x}, \Glyph{y}, \Glyph{z}]^\top\) satisfying \(\lVert \UnitQuaternion \rVert = 1\).
\(\Pose \in \SpecialEuclideanGroup\) Pose Matrix Rigid-body pose of a named frame, such as a grasp, the tool center point, or a target (\(\Glyph{\Pose_{\text{grasp}}}\), \(\Glyph{\Pose_{\text{TCP}}}\)); the transform between two frames is \(\HomogeneousTransform\) below.
\(\HomogeneousTransform \in \SpecialEuclideanGroup\) Homogeneous Transform Matrix \(4 \times 4\) matrix \(\begin{bmatrix} \TransformRotationBlock & \TransformTranslationBlock \\ \ZeroVector^\top & 1 \end{bmatrix}\) mapping coordinates from frame \(\Glyph{B}\) into frame \(\Glyph{A}\).
\(\AngularVelocity, \AngularAcceleration \in \mathbb{R}^3\) Angular Velocity & Acceleration \(\text{rad/s}, \text{rad/s}^2\) Spatial angular velocity and angular acceleration vectors of a rigid body.
\(\SensorLeverArm \in \mathbb{R}^3\) Sensor Lever-Arm Vector \(\text{m}\) Displacement vector from body rotation origin \(\Glyph{O}\) to displaced sensor location \(\Glyph{P}\); its length, or a link’s lever arm, is the scalar \(\LeverArmLength\).
\(\FundamentalMatrix, \EssentialMatrix\) Fundamental and Essential Matrices Matrix Two-view epipolar constraint \(\HomogeneousPixelCoordinates_2^\top \FundamentalMatrix\HomogeneousPixelCoordinates_1 = 0\) with \(\EssentialMatrix = [\RigidTransformTranslation]_\times \DirectionalCosineMatrix\); the subscript keeps it distinct from the transition matrix \(\StateTransitionMatrix\).
\(\CameraIntrinsicCalibrationMatrix \in \mathbb{R}^{3 \times 3}\) Pinhole Camera Intrinsics Pixels Calibration matrix \(\begin{bmatrix} \HorizontalFocalLength & 0 & \PrincipalPointColumn \\ 0 & \VerticalFocalLength & \PrincipalPointRow \\ 0 & 0 & 1 \end{bmatrix}\) parameterizing focal lengths and principal center.
\(\CameraIntrinsicInverseTranspose\) Inverse-transpose intrinsic matrix Matrix Transpose of the inverse of the camera intrinsic matrix \(\CameraIntrinsicCalibrationMatrix\), which carries an essential matrix into the pixel-coordinate fundamental matrix.
\(\CameraFramePoint = [\CameraFrameHorizontalCoordinate, \CameraFrameVerticalCoordinate, \CameraFrameDepth]^\top\) Camera-Frame 3D Coordinate \(\text{m}\) Metric coordinates of a spatial point in the optical camera frame (\(\CameraFrameDepth\) is optical depth).
\([\PixelColumnCoordinate, \PixelRowCoordinate]^\top\), \(\HomogeneousPixelCoordinates\) Image Plane Pixel Coordinates Pixels Discrete sensor pixel coordinates (\(\PixelColumnCoordinate \sim \HorizontalFocalLength \frac{\CameraFrameHorizontalCoordinate}{\CameraFrameDepth} + \PrincipalPointColumn\), \(\PixelRowCoordinate \sim \VerticalFocalLength \frac{\CameraFrameVerticalCoordinate}{\CameraFrameDepth} + \PrincipalPointRow\)), italic as scalars; the homogeneous image point is \(\HomogeneousPixelCoordinates = [\PixelColumnCoordinate, \PixelRowCoordinate, 1]^\top\), never bold \(\Glyph{\mathbf{u}}\).
\(\ExposureDuration\) Exposure Duration \(\text{ms}\) Integration time of one camera frame; the frame’s timestamp belongs at its midpoint, \(\ExposureDuration/2\) after exposure starts.
\(\BeliefInvalidationHorizon\) Belief Invalidation Horizon \(\text{ms}\) Time since the last supporting evidence after which a belief can no longer be shown to lie inside its task tolerance; a belief expires at \(\TimeInstant_0 + \BeliefInvalidationHorizon\).
\(\FreeSpaceDepth\) Free-Space Depth \(\text{m}\) Distance from a hull’s surface to the nearest cell that is occupied, unknown, or expired, read from an ESDF built with all three treated as obstacles; the budget motion may spend there (Volumetric metric maps and distance fields).
\(\RollingShutterRowReadout\) Rolling Shutter Row Readout \(\MicroUnitPrefix\text{s}\) Exposure time delta between adjacent CMOS sensor scanlines; row \(\SensorRowIndex\) starts at \(\TimeInstant(\SensorRowIndex) = \FrameExposureStartTime + \SensorRowIndex\,\RollingShutterRowReadout\).
