\(\ThrottleTripTemperature\) Throttle Trip Temperature °C Junction temperature at or above which a hardware thermal governor reduces the processor clock; paired with a lower clear temperature for hysteresis.
\(\TransistorThresholdVoltage\) Transistor Threshold Voltage \(\text{V}\) Gate-source voltage at which a transistor begins to conduct. The headroom between supply and threshold sets gate delay in the alpha-power law.
\(\VelocitySaturationExponent\) Velocity Saturation Exponent Dimensionless Technology-dependent exponent of the alpha-power law, by which drain current grows with gate overdrive above threshold; it falls from 2 for a long channel toward 1 as carrier velocity saturates.
\(\WindingCoreThermalResistance\) Winding-to-Core Thermal Resistance \(\text{K/W}\) Conductive thermal resistance from a motor winding to its stator core; in series with the core-to-ambient resistance it makes the total \(\ThermalResistance\).
\(\WindingCurrent\) Winding Current \(\text{A}\) Electric current flowing through a motor winding, which heats it at the rate of current squared times resistance.
\(\ConductorCurrent\) Conductor Current \(\text{A}\) Electrical current flowing through a power-distribution conductor; resistive loss is its square times the conductor resistance.
\(\WindingLossPower\) Winding Loss Power \(\text{W}\) Resistive power dissipated in the motor windings.
\(\WindingTemperatureLimit\) Winding Temperature Limit °C Largest winding temperature the insulation class and derating policy allow.
\(\MicroUnitPrefix\) Micro Unit Prefix Prefix SI prefix for one millionth, written before a unit such as seconds; it only scales the unit after it and is never a quantity, a service rate, or a mean.
\(\SupplyVoltage\) Supply Voltage \(\text{V}\) Voltage that powers a digital circuit and that voltage and frequency scaling lowers; dynamic power scales with \(\SupplyVoltage^2\).
\(\ParasiticLoopInductance\) Parasitic Loop Inductance \(\text{H}\) Unintended inductance of the current loop that feeds an accelerator core rail; it turns current slew into voltage droop.

Deep Learning Foundations and Edge Serving

Embodied robotic platforms increasingly run large multimodal foundation models, such as vision-language-action (VLA) backbones and cross-attention policy decoders, alongside deterministic real-time control stacks. Serving these models on an embedded edge computer requires tracking both the neural architecture dimensions and the memory footprint those dimensions occupy:

Symbol Definition Unit Notes
\(\AttentionHeadDimension\) Attention Head Dimension Integer Per-head hidden dimension in attention layers.
\(\HiddenDimension\) Hidden Dimension Integer Hidden feature (model) width \(\HiddenDimension\) of a transformer layer or representation; the distance to a target, also \(\Glyph{d}\), is \(\TargetTravelDistance\) and appears only in the intent-lease reachability test.
\(\ProbabilityFunction(\cdot)\) Distribution Distribution Probability mass or density \(\ProbabilityFunction(\Glyph{x})\), conditional \(\ProbabilityFunction(\Glyph{y} \mid \Glyph{x})\), or an observation model \(\ProbabilityFunction(\MultimodalObservation \mid \PhysicalWorldState)\); lowercase avoids the collision with \(\Glyph{P}\) (parameter count).
\(\Pr(\Glyph{E})\) Event Probability Dimensionless Probability of an event \(\Glyph{E}\), as in \(\Pr(\text{error by step } \Glyph{t})\); use \(\ProbabilityFunction(\cdot)\) for distributions.
\(\LayerInputWidth, \LayerOutputWidth\) Layer Input and Output Widths Integer Number of input and output features of a dense layer, which holds \(\LayerInputWidth \LayerOutputWidth\) weights.
\(\LearningRate\) Learning Rate Scalar Step size for the optimizer; never bare for hardware efficiency, which is \(\EfficiencyHw\).
\(\LossFunction\) Loss Function Scalar The objective minimized during training; a subscript or superscript names the objective (\(\Glyph{\LossFunction_{\text{MSE}}}\), \(\Glyph{\LossFunction_{\text{chunk}}}\), \(\Glyph{\LossFunction^{\text{CLIP}}}\)).
\(\ModelParameters\) Model Parameters Vector Trainable parameter vector or weights \(\ModelParameters\) of a learned model, such as a policy.
\(\LayerCount\) Number of Layers Integer Total number of layers in a network.
\(\SequenceLength\) Sequence Length tokens Input context or sequence length \(\SequenceLength\) in tokens. The behavioral-cloning horizon \(\BehaviorCloningHorizon\) also renders \(\Glyph{S}\) but counts control steps; the two never share an equation.
\(\WeightMemory\) Weight Memory bytes Memory occupied by model weights (\(\Parameters \times \text{bytes-per-parameter}\)).
\(\ElementStorageSize\) Element Storage Size bytes/element Bytes per stored tensor element at the deployed precision.
\(\ArithmeticIntensity\) Arithmetic Intensity FLOP/byte Operations per byte moved from off-chip memory, \(\Operations/\DataVolume\); it decides whether an evaluation is bound by memory or by compute.
\(\EffectiveSampleSize\) Effective Sample Size Dimensionless Number of independent observations that carry the same information about a mean as a serially correlated stream.
\(\EnvironmentParameters\) Environment Parameters Record External physical variables of one trial or rollout, such as floor friction, illuminance, obstacle geometry, and air temperature; written non-bold, so a record rather than a vector.
\(\EvaluationPredicate\) Evaluation Predicate Function Binary predicate that maps a realized trial trajectory to its task outcome, zero or one.
\(\ExceedanceReturnLevel\) Exceedance Return Level Varies Level of the monitored quantity exceeded with the target probability per sampled cycle; its subscript is that exceedance probability.
\(\ExceedanceThreshold\) Exceedance Threshold Varies High threshold on a performance metric above which peaks-over-threshold modeling fits exceedances; it carries the metric’s units (meters for a distance overshoot).
\(\GeneralizedParetoDistribution\) Generalized Pareto Distribution Function Cumulative distribution function of the excess over a high threshold, parameterized by a scale and a tail shape.
\(\GpdScaleParameter\) Generalized Pareto Scale Varies Scale parameter of the generalized Pareto distribution fitted to exceedances over a threshold.
\(\FailureUpperConfidenceLimit\) Failure Upper Confidence Limit Dimensionless One-sided upper confidence limit on the per-trial failure probability from a finite campaign.
\(\HypothesizedTrialFailureProbability\) Hypothesized Trial Failure Probability Dimensionless Per-trial failure probability a sequential probability ratio test specifies under one of its two hypotheses, indexed 0 for the unacceptable null and 1 for the acceptable alternative.
\(\HazardClassIndex\) Hazard Class Index Dimensionless Index of one defined hazard class.
\(\FleetRobotCount\) Fleet Robot Count Dimensionless Number of robots in a deployed fleet; not the accelerator count of a data center fleet.
\(\GaussianCovarianceMatrix\) Gaussian Covariance Matrix Matrix Covariance matrix of a multivariate Gaussian; entries carry the products of the component units.
\(\GaussianMeanVector\) Gaussian Mean Vector Vector Mean vector of a multivariate Gaussian, paired with \(\GaussianCovarianceMatrix\).
\(\PrefillOperations\) Prefill Operations \(\text{FLOP}\) Arithmetic operations one evaluation’s prefill executes over all of its input tokens; the compute-bound phase of a fresh evaluation, and an instance of the operation count.
\(\ProposerEvaluationRate\) Proposer Evaluation Rate \(\text{Hz}\) Rate of fresh proposer evaluations when every evaluation streams the weights.
\(\SoftmaxFunction\) Softmax Function Function Function that maps a vector of logits to a probability vector that sums to one; normalization by construction, not calibrated confidence, and not the sigmoid.
\(\ModelInput\) Model Input Record Input of one example, the data a model receives to produce a prediction; its label partner is \(\Glyph{\mathbf{y}}\). Not a scalar feature \(\Glyph{x_i}\) of a neuron.
\(\ModelPrediction\) Model Prediction Dimensionless Scalar prediction a model outputs for one example, compared with its label by the loss; the vector of class probabilities is \(\Glyph{\hat{\mathbf{y}}}\).
\(\LogitVector\) Logit Vector Vector Unnormalized score for every class that a classifier outputs before softmax; its entries are \(\OutputLogit\).
\(\OutputLogit\) Output Logit Dimensionless Raw score a model outputs for one class before the softmax, indexed by class as \(\Glyph{z_i}\).
\(\ValidationLoss\) Validation Loss Scalar Loss a trained model incurs on held-out validation data; the outer objective of neural architecture search, never the training loss.
\(\WeightMatrix\) Weight Matrix Matrix Matrix of learned weights one layer multiplies its input by, or the kernel tensor of a convolution layer; a subscript names the layer, as in \(\Glyph{\mathbf{W}_1}\).
\(\FunctionJacobian\) Function Jacobian Matrix Matrix of partial derivatives of a differentiated function’s outputs with respect to its inputs; not the manipulator Jacobian of a robot arm.
\(\ZeroMatrix\) Zero Matrix or Vector Matrix Matrix or vector whose entries are all zero; bold, so it is not the scalar zero.
\(\ImageHeight\) Image Height pixels Number of pixel rows in an image or its segmentation mask; shares its glyph with horizons but counts rows.
\(\ImageWidth\) Image Width pixels Number of pixel columns in an image or its segmentation mask.
\(\IndicatorFunction\) Indicator Function Function Function equal to one when its condition holds and zero otherwise.
\(\KlDivergence\) KL Divergence nats or bits Expected extra code length from encoding one distribution with a code built for another; asymmetric, so \(\Glyph{\KlDivergence(p \lVert q) \ne \KlDivergence(q \lVert p)}\), and distillation uses the teacher-to-student order.
\(\ShannonEntropy\) Shannon Entropy bits Average uncertainty of a probability distribution, the expected negative log probability of its outcomes; the log base sets the unit, base two for bits and natural log for nats.
\(\StatisticalHypothesis\) Statistical Hypothesis Record A hypothesis about a population: \(\Glyph{H_0}\) the null, \(\Glyph{H_1}\) the alternative, and \(\Glyph{H_{(i)}}\) the hypothesis with the \(\Glyph{i}\)-th smallest p-value. A record, not a number.
\(\SignificanceCriticalQuantile\) Significance Critical Quantile Dimensionless Standard-normal quantile at one minus half the significance level, the two-sided critical value of a release test; about 1.96 at a significance level of 0.05.
\(\NextDecisionProcessState\) Next Decision-Process State Record State a decision process moves to after an action is taken in the current state, as in the transition law \(\Glyph{p(s^\prime \mid s, a)}\); the successor of the current state.
\(\PolicyObservedState\) Policy Observed State Record State or observation a policy conditions its choice of action on, the conditioning argument in \(\Glyph{\pi_\theta(a \mid s)}\).
\(\PolicyAction\) Policy Action Record Action a policy chooses given the state it observes, the chosen argument in \(\Glyph{\pi_\theta(a \mid s)}\); agentic rollouts use their own rollout-action symbol.
\(\PolicyProbabilityRatio\) Policy Probability Ratio Dimensionless Ratio of a sampled token’s probability under the current policy to its probability under the behavior policy that generated it; clipped to an interval of half-width \(\RatioClippingWidth\) around one.
\(\RatioClippingWidth\) Ratio Clipping Width Dimensionless Half-width of the interval around one to which the clipped surrogate limits the probability ratio; between zero and one.
\(\PolicyHistoryActionReturn\) Policy History-Action Return Dimensionless Expected remaining return under the current policy after taking a given action at a given interaction history and then following that policy, written \(\Glyph{Q^{\pi}(h_k, a_k)}\).
\(\PolicyRemainingReturn\) Policy Remaining Return Dimensionless Expected remaining return under the current policy from a given interaction history, written \(\Glyph{V^{\pi}(h_k)}\); the baseline the advantage subtracts.
\(\PolicyHistoryAdvantage\) Policy History Advantage Dimensionless Return of a given action at a given interaction history minus the remaining return at that history, \(\Glyph{\PolicyHistoryAdvantage(h_k, a_k) = \PolicyHistoryActionReturn(h_k, a_k) - \PolicyRemainingReturn(h_k)}\).
\(\StateDistribution\) State Distribution Distribution Average distribution of states a policy visits over the horizon; the per-step form carries a superscript, \(\Glyph{d_\pi^t}\).
\(\ReferenceStateDistribution\) Reference State Distribution Distribution Average distribution of states the reference policy visits over the horizon; the reference-policy counterpart of \(\StateDistribution\).
\(\Trajectory\) Trajectory Record Ordered record of the states, actions, and observations of one delegated task, \(\Glyph{\tau = (s_0, a_0, o_1, \dots, s_H)}\); a record, not a number.
\(\ClassCount\) Class Count Count Number of classes a classifier distinguishes, or vocabulary entries it scores; the length of its softmax output.
\(\ExampleLabel\) Example Label Record Label paired with one input, treated as a random variable; a bold form is the label written as a vector.

Latin matrices and vectors are set in bold (\(\Glyph{\mathbf{W}}\), \(\Glyph{\mathbf{x}}\)); scalars, dimensions, indices, and individual matrix entries stay italic (\(\Glyph{N}\), \(\Glyph{d}\), \(\Glyph{i}\), \(\Glyph{W}_{ij}\)). The generic parameter symbol \(\Glyph{\theta}\) is the one conventional exception and stays italic. Local matrix algebra follows the same bold rule: matrix operands are bold (\(\Glyph{\mathbf{A}}\), \(\Glyph{\mathbf{B}}\), \(\Glyph{\mathbf{C}}\)), as in general matrix multiply (GEMM) \(\Glyph{\mathbf{C}}=\Glyph{\alpha} \Glyph{\mathbf{A}}\Glyph{\mathbf{B}}+\Glyph{\beta} \Glyph{\mathbf{C}}\). The bold form marks a matrix operand and is distinct from the italic scalar batch size \(\BatchSize\), which remains batch size in scalar contexts. A GEMM’s scalar dimensions are \(\Glyph{M}\), \(\Glyph{N}\), and \(\Glyph{K}\) (\(\Glyph{\mathbf{A}}\) is \(\Glyph{M} \times \Glyph{K}\), \(\Glyph{\mathbf{B}}\) is \(\Glyph{K} \times \Glyph{N}\)). Asymptotic cost is written \(\mathcal{O}(\cdot)\), which keeps it distinct from the operation count \(\Operations\).

Evaluation and Statistical Evidence

A physical trial is expensive, so the evaluation chapters count carefully. These symbols name the trials themselves, the samples a metric is computed over, and the threshold exceedances that tail estimates are built from.

Symbol Definition Unit Notes
\(\CalibrationExampleCount\) Calibration Example Count Count Number of held-out calibration examples, and so of nonconformity scores, used to set a conformal prediction threshold. Not the quantization calibration set.
\(\EvaluationSampleCount\) Evaluation Sample Count Count Number of examples in the evaluation set over which an accuracy or calibration metric is computed; it sets the sampling error. Not the training set size \(\Glyph{D}\).
\(\DiscreteRandomVariable\) Discrete Random Variable Record Discrete random variable, the uncertain quantity whose outcomes a distribution describes; uppercase for the variable, lowercase for one of its outcomes.
\(\TrialCondition\) Trial Condition Record Condition of one physical trial: initial plant state, environment parameters, plant parameters, computational configuration, and termination protocol; drawn from the operational test distribution.
\(\TrialIndex\) Trial Index Index Position of one physical trial within a learning campaign, counted from one.
\(\TrialSuccessProbability\) Trial Success Probability Dimensionless True probability that one trial succeeds under the declared evaluation protocol and test distribution.
\(\ThresholdExceedanceCount\) Threshold Exceedance Count Count Number of trials whose performance metric exceeds the threshold.
\(\ThresholdExcess\) Threshold Excess Varies Amount by which the monitored quantity exceeds the threshold, one recorded exceedance; it carries the unit of the monitored quantity. Its random-variable form is \(\ThresholdExcessVariable\).
\(\ThresholdExcessVariable\) Threshold Excess Variable Varies Excess of the monitored quantity over the threshold, conditional on exceeding it, treated as a random variable.

Units and Precision

  • Physical units: This book uses SI (metric) units throughout, including meters, kilograms, seconds, watts, and °C, consistent with standard engineering and scientific practice. Where source data was originally reported in imperial units, the book converts to SI and notes the original values parenthetically. A space always separates the number from the unit (for example, 100 ms, 2 TB/s).
  • Data and memory: This book uses decimal SI prefixes only: KB = \(10^3\) bytes, MB = \(10^6\), GB = \(10^9\), TB = \(10^{12}\). Binary-prefixed units do not appear in prose; model sizes, activation footprints, throughputs, and transfer volumes are reported in decimal units (for example, 2 TB/s, 102 MB). Hardware memory capacities are the one exception: they keep the vendor’s nameplate figure and label. A device modeled here with a binary 80 GB capacity holds \(80 \times 2^{30}\) bytes; usable capacity and allocation headroom remain separate.
  • Compute: The notation distinguishes operation counts from rates. Total work uses FLOPs (for example, GFLOPs, TFLOPs), while throughput uses FLOP/s with decimal prefixes (for example, GFLOP/s, TFLOP/s).
    • 1 TFLOP = \(10^{12}\) FLOPs
    • 1 TFLOP/s = \(10^{12}\) FLOPs per second
    • Arithmetic intensity conventionally uses FLOP/byte as a unit ratio (floating-point operations per byte moved). This is a ratio unit, not a total-work symbol or throughput symbol.
  • Power: Power carries a subscript (\(\Glyph{P_{\text{avg}}}\), \(\Glyph{P_{\text{device}}}\), \(\Glyph{P_{\text{cluster}}}\)); bare \(\Glyph{P}\) remains parameter count.
  • Latency percentiles: Written p50, p95, and p99 in upright type, inside math as well as in prose.
  • Currency: Dollar amounts use the dollar sign ($); unless otherwise noted, dollar-denominated costs are U.S. dollars (USD).
  • Precision:
    • FP64: Double precision (8 bytes)
    • FP32: Single precision (4 bytes)
    • TF32: TensorFloat-32 (19-bit Tensor Core compute mode; inputs and storage remain FP32)
    • FP16: Half precision (2 bytes, standard range)
    • BF16: Brain float (2 bytes, wide dynamic range)
    • FP8: Quarter precision (1 byte, E4M3 or E5M2 format)
    • FP4: 4-bit floating-point format
    • INT8: 8-bit integer (1 byte)
    • INT4: 4-bit integer; lower integer precisions follow the same uppercase INTn pattern (for example, INT3, INT2)

Quick Reference: Resolving Collisions

Throughout this book, two single-letter symbols recur in both machine learning equations and hardware performance budgets. The most frequent symbol collisions across the machine learning, control, and embedded systems literatures are resolved as follows:

Symbol ML Meaning Systems Meaning Book Convention
\(\BatchSize\) Batch Size Bandwidth Batch Size. Use \(\Bandwidth\) for bandwidth.
\(\Parameters\) Parameters Peak FLOP/s Parameters. Use \(\PeakRate\) for peak rate.

As a general principle, ML conventions take precedence for single letters, while systems concepts get subscripts or multi-letter symbols. This preserves compatibility with the ML literature while distinguishing the robotics and embedded systems meanings used in this book.
